j=1,\ldots,m\end{subarray}}\sup_{\mathbf{u}\in\overline{B}_{\mathcal{H}}}\Big|\Big\langle A_{\mathbf{Y},E^{\prime}}\mathbf{u},\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}(A_{\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\prime},E}v_{j})\Big\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\Big|

\displaystyle=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}v_{j}\in\overline{B}_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}\\
j=1,\ldots,m\end{subarray}}\sup_{\mathbf{u}\in\overline{B}_{\mathcal{H}}}\Big|\Big\langle A_{\mathbf{Y},E^{\prime}}\mathbf{u},\Big(\bigotimes_{i=1}^{m}A_{\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\prime},E}\Big)\Big(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}v_{j}\Big)\Big\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\Big|

\displaystyle=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}v_{j}\in\overline{B}_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}\\
j=1,\ldots,m\end{subarray}}\sup_{\mathbf{u}\in\overline{B}_{\mathcal{H}}}\Big|\Big\langle\mathbf{u},A_{\mathbf{Y},E^{\prime}}\Big(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}A_{\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\prime},E}\Big)\Big(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}v_{j}\Big)\Big\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\Big|

\displaystyle=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}v_{j}\in\overline{B}_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}\\
j=1,\ldots,m\end{subarray}}\Big\|A_{\mathbf{Y},E^{\prime}}\Big(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}A_{\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\prime},E}\Big)\Big(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}v_{j}\Big)\Big\|_{\mathcal{H}}\overset{\eqref{eq:norm_of_matrix_columns}}{\eqsim_{\vec{n}}}\Big\|A_{\mathbf{Y},E^{\prime}}\Big(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}A_{\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\prime},E}\Big)\Big\|_{\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{H}}.

Conversely, assume that
\ind_{E}v\in\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\prime}
, for all
v\in\mathcal{H}_{j}
, for all
j=1,\ldots,m
and
\ind_{E^{\prime}}\mathbf{u}\in\mathbf{Y}
, for all
\mathbf{u}\in\mathcal{H}
. First of all, observe that for all
j=1,\ldots,m
, since
\ind_{E}v\in\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\prime}
, for all
v\in\mathcal{H}_{j}
, exactly as in the proof of part (b) of
[
Nie24b
, Proposition 4.1]
we have that every
f\in\mathbf{X}_{j}
is integrable over
E
.

Let now
f_{j}\in\mathbf{X}_{j}
,
j=1,\ldots,m
be arbitrary. Set

C:=\Big\|A_{\mathbf{Y},E^{\prime}}\Big(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}A_{\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\prime},E}\Big)\Big\|_{\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{H}}.

We estimate

\displaystyle\mu(E)^{m}\|T_{E,E^{\prime}}(f_{1},\ldots,f_{m})\|_{\mathbf{Y}}=\mu(E)^{m}\Big\|\ind_{E^{\prime}}\Big(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}\langle f_{j}\rangle_{E}\Big)\Big\|_{\mathbf{Y}}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:reducing_matrix}}{\lesssim_{K_{\mathbf{Y}},\vec{n}}}\mu(E)^{m}C\Big\|\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}(A_{\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\prime},E}^{-1}\langle f_{j}\rangle_{E})\Big\|_{\mathcal{H}}=C\prod_{j=1}^{m}\Big\|\int_{E}A_{\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\prime},E}^{-1}f_{j}(x)\mathrm{d}\mu(x)\Big\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}.

For each
j=1,\ldots,m
, choosing an orthonormal basis
e_{1},\ldots,e_{n_{j}}
of
\mathcal{H}_{j}
we get

\displaystyle\Big\|\int_{E}A_{\mathbf{X}_{j},E}^{\prime}f_{j}(x)\mathrm{d}\mu(x)\Big\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}=\sum_{k=1}^{n_{j}}\Big|\Big\langle\int_{E}A_{\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\prime},E}^{-1}f_{j}(x)\mathrm{d}\mu(x),e_{k}\Big\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}\Big|^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{n_{j}}\Big|\int_{E}\langle f_{j}(x),A_{\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\prime},E}^{-1}e_{k}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}\mathrm{d}\mu(x)\Big|^{2}\leq\|f\|_{\mathbf{X}_{j}}^{2}\sum_{k=1}^{n_{j}}\|\ind_{E}A_{\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\prime},E}^{-1}e_{k}\|_{\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\prime}}^{2}

\displaystyle\eqsim_{n_{j}}\|f\|_{\mathbf{X}_{j}}^{2}\sum_{k=1}^{n_{j}}\|A_{\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\prime},E}A_{\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\prime},E}^{-1}e_{k}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}^{2}=n_{j}\|f\|_{\mathbf{X}_{j}}^{2}.

Therefore, we deduce

\mu(E)^{m}\|T_{E,E^{\prime}}(\vec{f})\|_{\mathbf{Y}}\lesssim_{\vec{n}}C\prod_{j=1}^{m}\|f_{j}\|_{\mathbf{X}_{j}}.

Thus,
T_{E}:\mathbf{X}_{1}\times\ldots\times\mathbf{X}_{m}\to\mathbf{Y}
.
∎

Using Lemma
2.6
in the special case of weighted Lebesgue spaces, we regain the familiar expression featuring integral averages. To state it, we denote by
A_{W,E,p}
the reducing operator in
L^{p}_{W}\Big(\Omega,\frac{\mu}{\mu(E)}\Big)
of
E
for
p\in(0,\infty]
(where we note that the quasi-Banach function space
L^{p}(\Omega,\frac{\mu}{\mu(E)})
has the Fatou property). Observe that

K_{L^{p}\Big(\Omega,\frac{\mu}{\mu(E)}\Big)}=2^{(\frac{1}{p}-1)_{+}}=:K_{p},\quad\forall p\in(0,\infty].

Proposition 3.2
.

Let
\vec{t}\in(0,\infty]^{m}
,
q\in(0,\infty]
and let
\vec{W}
be a
m
-tuple of matrix weights. Then, the following are equivalent:

(1)

W_{j}^{-1}
is
t_{j}
-integrable over
E
, for all
j=1,\ldots,m
and
\mathbf{W}
is
q
-integrable over
E^{\prime}

(2)

C:=\Big(\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptE^{\prime}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{E^{\prime}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{E^{\prime}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{E^{\prime}}}\displaystyle\prod_{j=1}^{m}\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptE}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{E}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{E}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{E}}\|W_{j}(y)W_{j}(x)^{-1}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}\to\mathcal{H}_{j}}^{t_{j}}\mathrm{d}\mu(x)\Big)^{\frac{q}{t_{j}}}\,\mathrm{d}\mu(y)\Big)^{\frac{1}{q}}<\infty

(where the usual interpretations are in force if
t_{j}=\infty
for some
j
or
q=\infty
).
Moreover, in this case we have

(3.5)

C\eqsim_{\vec{n},K_{t_{1}},\ldots,K_{t_{m}},K_{q}}\Big\|A_{\mathbf{W},E^{\prime},q}\Big(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}A_{W_{j}^{-1},E,t_{j}}\Big)\Big\|_{\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{H}}.

We note that if
t_{j}<\infty
, then the condition that
W_{j}^{-1}
is
t_{j}
-integrable over
E
means

\int_{E}\|W_{j}(x)^{-1}\|^{t_{j}}_{\mathcal{H}_{j}\to\mathcal{H}_{j}}\mathrm{d}\mu(x)<\infty,

or equivalently (by the spectral theorem for positive definite operators on a Hilbert space)

\int_{E}\|W_{j}(x)^{-t_{j}}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}\to\mathcal{H}_{j}}\mathrm{d}\mu(x)<\infty,

that is,
W_{j}^{-t_{j}}
is integrable over
E
. Similarly, if
q<\infty
, then the condition that
\mathbf{W}
is
q
-integrable over
E^{\prime}
is equivalent to the condition that
\mathbf{W}^{q}
is integrable over
E^{\prime}
.

Proof of Proposition
3.2
.

First of all, if
1
is true, then the reducing matrices in (
3.5
) are well-defined. Thus, using Lemma
2.6
, we have

\displaystyle C

\displaystyle=\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptE^{\prime}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{E^{\prime}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{E^{\prime}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{E^{\prime}}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptE}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{E}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{E}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{E}}\|W_{j}(y)W_{j}(x)^{-1}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}\to\mathcal{H}_{j}}^{t_{j}}\,\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{\frac{q}{t_{j}}}\,\mathrm{d}y\Big)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle=\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptE^{\prime}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{E^{\prime}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{E^{\prime}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{E^{\prime}}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptE}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{E}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{E}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{E}}\|W_{j}(x)^{-1}W_{j}(y)\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}\to\mathcal{H}_{j}}^{t_{j}}\,\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{\frac{q}{t_{j}}}\,\mathrm{d}y\Big)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\eqsim_{\vec{n},K_{t_{1}},\ldots,K_{t_{m}}}\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptE^{\prime}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{E^{\prime}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{E^{\prime}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{E^{\prime}}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\|A_{W_{j}^{-1},E,t_{j}}W_{j}(y)\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}\to\mathcal{H}_{j}}^{q}\,\mathrm{d}y\Big)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle=\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptE^{\prime}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{E^{\prime}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{E^{\prime}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{E^{\prime}}}\Big\|\Big(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}A_{W_{j}^{-1},E,t_{j}}\Big)\mathbf{W}(y)\Big\|_{\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{H}}^{q}\mathrm{d}\mu(y)\Big)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle=\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptE^{\prime}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{E^{\prime}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{E^{\prime}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{E^{\prime}}}\Big\|\mathbf{W}(y)\Big(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}A_{W_{j}^{-1},E,t_{j}}\Big)\Big\|_{\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{H}}^{q}\,\mathrm{d}y\Big)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\eqsim_{n,K_{q}}\Big\|A_{\mathbf{W},E^{\prime},q}\Big(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}A_{W_{j}^{-1},E,t_{j}}\Big)\Big\|_{\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{H}}.

Conversely, assume that
C<\infty
. Then, there is
y_{0}\in E
such that
\mathbf{W}_{j}(y_{0})
is invertible, for all
j=1,\ldots,m
and

\prod_{j=1}^{m}\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptE}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{E}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{E}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{E}}\|W_{j}(y_{0})W_{j}(x)^{-1}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{i}\to\mathcal{H}_{j}}^{t_{j}}\mathrm{d}\mu(x)\Big)^{\frac{q}{t_{j}}}<\infty.

Then

\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptE}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{E}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{E}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{E}}\|W_{j}(y_{0})W_{j}(x)^{-1}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}\to\mathcal{H}_{j}}^{t_{j}}\mathrm{d}\mu(x)<\infty,\quad\forall j=1,\ldots,m.

Since
W_{j}(y_{0})
is a fixed invertible linear operator on
\mathcal{H}_{j}
, we deduce that
W_{j}^{-1}
is
t_{j}
-integrable over
E
for all
j=1,\ldots,m
. Then, similarly to previously, we have

\displaystyle\infty>C\eqsim_{\vec{n},K_{t_{1}},\ldots,K_{t_{m}}}\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptE^{\prime}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{E^{\prime}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{E^{\prime}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{E^{\prime}}}\Big\|\Big(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}A_{W_{j}^{-1},E,t_{j}}\Big)\mathbf{W}(y)\Big\|_{\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{H}}^{q}\,\mathrm{d}y\Big)^{\frac{1}{q}}.

Since
\displaystyle\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}A_{W_{j}^{-1},E,t_{j}}\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{H})
is a fixed invertible linear operator on
\mathcal{H}
, we deduce that
\mathbf{W}
is
q
-integrable over
E^{\prime}
, concluding the proof.
∎

Finally, we prove a result that shows that the linear matrix Muckenhoupt classes embed into our bilinear matrix Muckenhoupt weight classes.

Proposition 3.3
.

Let
X
be a Banach function space over
\Omega
with the Fatou property and let
W:\Omega\to\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{H})
be a matrix weight. Then the following are equivalent:

(1)

T_{E}:X_{W}\to X_{W}
;

(2)

T_{E}:(X^{\prime})_{W^{-1}}\to(X^{\prime})_{W^{-1}}
;

(3)

T_{E}:X_{W}\times(X^{\prime})_{W^{-1}}\to L^{1}_{W\otimes W^{-1}}(\Omega;\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathcal{H})
.

Moreover, in this case we have

\|T_{E}\|_{X_{W}\to X_{W}}=\|T_{E}\|_{(X^{\prime})_{W^{-1}}\to(X^{\prime})_{W^{-1}}}\leq\|T_{E}\|_{X_{W}\times(X^{\prime})_{W^{-1}}\to L^{1}_{W\otimes W^{-1}}(\Omega;\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathcal{H})},

\|T_{E}\|_{X_{W}\times(X^{\prime})_{W^{-1}}\to L^{1}_{W\otimes W^{-1}}(\Omega;\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathcal{H})}\leq\|T_{E}\|_{X_{W}\to X_{W}}\|T_{E}\|_{(X^{\prime})_{W^{-1}}\to(X^{\prime})_{W^{-1}}}.

Proof.

The equivalence
1
\Leftrightarrow
2
with equal norm follows from (
2.1
) and the fact that

\int_{\Omega}\langle T_{E}f,g\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\,\mathrm{d}\mu=\int_{\Omega}\langle f,T_{E}g\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\,\mathrm{d}\mu.

For
3
\Rightarrow
1
, note that for any
g\in(X^{\prime})_{W^{-1}}
we have

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{\Omega}\!\langle T_{E}f,g\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\,\mathrm{d}\mu\Big|

\displaystyle=\Big|\int_{E}\!\langle\langle f\rangle_{E},\langle g\rangle_{E}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\,\mathrm{d}\mu\Big|=\Big|\int_{E}\!\langle W(x)\langle f\rangle_{E},W(x)^{-1}\langle g\rangle_{E}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\,\mathrm{d}\mu\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{E}\!\|W(x)\langle f\rangle_{E}\|_{\mathcal{H}}\|W(x)^{-1}\langle g\rangle_{E}\|_{\mathcal{H}}\,\mathrm{d}\mu=\|T_{E}(f,g)\|_{L^{1}_{W\otimes W^{-1}}(\Omega;\mathbf{F}^{n}\otimes\mathbf{F}^{n})}

\displaystyle\leq\|T_{E}\|_{X_{W}\times(X^{\prime})_{W^{-1}}\to L^{1}_{W\otimes W^{-1}}(\Omega;\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathcal{H})}\|f\|_{X_{W}}\|g\|_{(X^{\prime})_{W^{-1}}}

Taking a supremum over all
g\in(X^{\prime})_{W^{-1}}=(X_{W})^{\prime}
of norm
1
proves that
T_{E}:X_{W}\to X_{W}
, with

\|T_{E}\|_{X_{W}\to X_{W}}\leq\|T_{E}\|_{X_{W}\times(X^{\prime})_{W^{-1}}\to L^{1}_{W\otimes W^{-1}}(\Omega;\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathcal{H})}.

For
1
&
2
\Rightarrow
3
, note that

\displaystyle\|T_{E}(f,g)\|_{L^{1}_{W\otimes W^{-1}}(\Omega;\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathcal{H})}

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\|W(x)T_{E}f(x)\|_{\mathcal{H}}\|W(x)^{-1}T_{E}g(x)\|_{\mathcal{H}}\,\mathrm{d}\mu

\displaystyle\leq\|T_{E}f\|_{X_{W}}\|T_{E}g\|_{(X^{\prime})_{W^{-1}}},

which shows that

\|T_{E}\|_{X_{W}\times(X^{\prime})_{W^{-1}}\to L^{1}_{W\otimes W^{-1}}(\Omega;\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathcal{H})}\leq\|T_{E}\|_{X_{W}\to X_{W}}\|T_{E}\|_{(X^{\prime})_{W^{-1}}\to(X^{\prime})_{W^{-1}}},

as desired.
∎

Remark 3.4
.

When
\mathcal{H}=\mathbf{F}
, we have
L^{1}_{W\otimes W^{-1}}(\Omega;\mathbf{F}\otimes\mathbf{F})\cong L^{1}(\Omega)
under the canonical identification
\mathbf{F}\otimes\mathbf{F}=\mathbf{F}
. Now, in the implication
1
&
2
\Rightarrow
3
we can use the fact that

\int_{\Omega}T_{E}(f,g)(x)\,\mathrm{d}\mu=\int_{\Omega}T_{E}f(x)g(x)\,\mathrm{d}\mu

to produce a bound using only
T_{E}:X_{W}\to X_{W}
. Thus, in this case we have

\|T_{E}\|_{X_{W}\times(X^{\prime})_{W^{-1}}\to L^{1}(\Omega)}=\|T_{E}\|_{X_{W}\to X_{W}}=\|T_{E}\|_{(X^{\prime})_{W^{-1}}\to(X^{\prime})_{W^{-1}}}.

It is unclear whether this same equality holds for general
\mathcal{H}
or not.

4.
The multilinear matrix Muckenhoupt condition

Let
\vec{W}
be an
m
-tuple of matrix weights and let
\vec{p}=(p_{1},\ldots,p_{m})\in[1,\infty]^{m}
. We set

L^{\vec{p}}_{\vec{W}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\vec{\mathcal{H}}):=L^{p_{1}}_{W_{1}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{H}_{1})\times\cdots\times L^{p_{m}}_{W_{m}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{H}_{m}),

and, recalling our conventions, we have defined

\mathcal{H}:=\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}\mathcal{H}_{j},\quad\mathbf{W}:=\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}W_{j}:\mathbf{R}^{d}\to\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{H}),\quad\frac{1}{p}:=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{1}{p_{j}}.

We say that
\vec{W}
satisfies the Muckenhoupt
A_{\vec{p}}
condition and write
\vec{W}\in A_{\vec{p}}
, if
W_{j}^{-1}
is locally
p_{j}^{\prime}
-integrable for all
j=1,\ldots,m
and

[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p}}:=\sup_{Q}\|T_{Q}\|_{L^{\vec{p}}_{\vec{W}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\vec{\mathcal{H}})\to L^{p}_{\mathbf{W}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{H})}<\infty,

where the supremum is taken over all cubes
Q\subseteq\mathbf{R}^{d}
.

Remark 4.1
.

The condition that
W_{j}^{-1}
is locally
p_{j}^{\prime}
-integrable ensures that any function
f\in L^{p_{j}}_{W_{j}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{H}_{j})
is locally integrable.

Definition 4.2
.

Let
\vec{W}
be a
m
-tuple of matrix weights. Let also
\vec{p}\in(0,\infty]^{m}
,
\vec{r}\in(0,\infty)^{m}
and
s\in(\mathbf{R}\backslash\{0\})\cup\{\infty\}
with
\vec{p}\geq\vec{r}
(i.e.
p_{j}\geq r_{j}
for all
j=1,\ldots,m
) and
\frac{1}{p}\geq\frac{1}{s}
(where by convention
\frac{1}{\infty}:=0
). Define

\frac{1}{q}:=\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{s}\in[0,\infty),\quad\frac{1}{t_{j}}:=\frac{1}{r_{j}}-\frac{1}{p_{j}}\in[0,\infty)\quad\text{ for $j=1,\ldots,m$}.

We define

[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p},(\vec{r},s),\mathrm{op}}:=\sup_{Q}\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}\!\|W_{j}(x)W_{j}(x_{j})^{-1}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}\to\mathcal{H}_{j}}^{t_{j}}\,\mathrm{d}x_{j}\Big)^{\frac{q}{t_{j}}}\,\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{\frac{1}{q}},

where the supremum is taken over all cubes
Q\subseteq\mathbf{R}^{d}
, and an integral average is interpreted as an essential supremum whenever the associated exponent is infinite. In particular, if
\vec{p}\in[1,\infty]^{m}
,
\vec{r}=(1,\ldots,1)
and
s=\infty
(so that
q=p
and
t_{j}=p_{j}^{\prime}
for
j=1,\ldots,m
), then we define

[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p},\mathrm{op}}:=[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p},(\vec{r},s),\mathrm{op}}.

Proposition 4.3
.

Let
\vec{p}\in[1,\infty]^{m}
and let
\vec{W}
be an
m
-tuple of matrix weights. Then, we have
\vec{W}\in A_{\vec{p}}
if and only if
[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p},\mathrm{op}}<\infty
. Moreover, in this case we have

[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p},\mathrm{op}}\lesssim_{\vec{n},m}[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p}}\leq[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p},\mathrm{op}}.

Proof.

First suppose that
[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p},\text{op}}<\infty
. Then, by Proposition
3.2
, we have that
W_{j}^{-1}
is locally
p_{j}^{\prime}
-integrable for all
j=1,\ldots,m
. Moreover, we estimate

\displaystyle\|T_{Q}(\vec{f})\|_{L^{p}_{\mathbf{W}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{H})}

\displaystyle=\Big\|\prod_{j=1}^{m}|W_{j}\langle W_{j}^{-1}W_{j}f_{j}\rangle_{Q}|\ind_{Q}\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbf{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\int_{Q}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}\!\|W_{j}(x)W_{j}(x_{j})^{-1}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}\to\mathcal{H}_{j}}\|W_{j}(x_{j})f_{j}(x_{j})\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}\,\mathrm{d}x_{j}\Big)^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\int_{Q}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}\!\|W_{j}(x)W_{j}(x_{j})^{-1}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}\to\mathcal{H}_{j}}^{p_{j}^{\prime}}\,\mathrm{d}x_{j}\Big)^{\frac{p}{p_{j}^{\prime}}}\langle\|W_{j}f_{j}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}\rangle^{p}_{p_{j},Q}\,\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p},\text{op}}|Q|^{\frac{1}{p}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\langle\|W_{j}f_{j}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}\rangle_{p_{j},Q}\leq[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p},\text{op}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\|f_{j}\|_{L^{p_{j}}_{W_{j}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{H}_{j})}.

Taking a supremum over all cubes
Q\subseteq\mathbf{R}^{d}
proves that
\vec{W}\in A_{\vec{p}}
with
[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p}}\leq[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p},\text{op}}
.

The converse follows immediately from Proposition
3.1
coupled with Proposition
3.2
, (
2.5
) and the observation that for any measure space
(\Omega,\mu)
we have
K_{L^{p}(\Omega,\mu)}\leq 2^{m-1}
.
∎

The main result of this section is the following:

Theorem 4.4
.

Let
\vec{p}\in[1,\infty]^{m}
, and let
\vec{W}\in A_{\vec{p}}
. Then we have

\sup_{v\in\mathcal{H}\setminus\{0\}}[\|\mathbf{W}v\|_{\mathcal{H}}]_{p}\leq[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p},\mathrm{op}},

and

\sup_{v\in\mathcal{H}_{j}\setminus\{0\}}[\|W_{j}^{-1}v\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}]_{p_{j}^{\prime}}\lesssim_{m,\vec{n}}[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p},\mathrm{op}},\quad\forall j=1,\ldots,m.

In particular, if
p<\infty
, we have

[\mathbf{W}]_{\mathrm{FW}_{p}}:=\sup_{v\in\mathcal{H}\setminus\{0\}}[\|\mathbf{W}v\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{p}]_{\mathrm{FW}}^{\frac{1}{p}}\lesssim_{m,p}[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p},\mathrm{op}}

and, if
p_{j}>1
,

[W_{j}^{-1}]_{\mathrm{FW}_{p_{j}^{\prime}}}:=\sup_{v\in\mathcal{H}_{j}\setminus\{0\}}[\|W_{j}^{-1}v\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}^{p^{\prime}_{j}}]_{\mathrm{FW}}^{\frac{1}{p_{j}^{\prime}}}\lesssim_{d,m,\vec{n},p_{j}}[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p},\mathrm{op}}

for all
j=1,\ldots,m
.

We defer the proof to the end of this section.

The following result is a multilinear analogue of the result that the norms of an
A_{p}
matrix weight applied to vectors, uniformly belong to the scalar
A_{p}
class.

Proposition 4.5
.

Let
\vec{p}\in[1,\infty]^{m}
and let
\vec{W}
be an
m
-tuple of matrix weights. If
\vec{W}\in A_{\vec{p}}
, then

\vec{W}\vec{u}:=(\|W_{1}u_{1}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{1}},\ldots,\|W_{m}u_{m}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{m}})\in A_{\vec{p}}

for all
u_{1},\ldots,u_{m}
with
u_{j}\in\mathcal{H}_{j}\backslash\{0\}
. Moreover, we have

\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}u_{1},\ldots,u_{m}\\
u_{j}\in\mathcal{H}_{j}\backslash\{0\}\end{subarray}}[\vec{W}\vec{u}]_{\vec{p}}\leq[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p}}.

Proof.

Observe that

\|u_{j}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}^{2}\|W_{j}u_{j}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}^{-1}\leq\|W_{j}^{-1}u_{j}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}},

so
\|W_{j}u_{j}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}^{-1}
is locally
p_{j}^{\prime}
-integrable. Let
h_{j}\in L^{p_{j}}_{\|W_{j}u_{j}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
. Then
f_{j}:=h_{j}u_{j}\in L^{p_{j}}_{W_{j}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{H}_{j})
with

\|f_{j}\|_{L^{p_{j}}_{W_{j}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{H}_{j})}=\|h_{j}\|_{L^{p_{j}}_{\|W_{j}u_{j}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}}(\mathbf{R}^{d})},

and

T_{Q}(\vec{f})=\Big(\prod_{j=1}^{m}\langle h_{j}\rangle_{Q}\ind_{Q}\Big)\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}u_{j}=T_{Q}(\vec{h})\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}u_{j}.

Hence, since
\Big\|\mathbf{W}\Big(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}u_{j}\Big)\Big\|_{\mathcal{H}}=\prod_{j=1}^{m}\|W_{j}u_{j}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}=:W_{\vec{u}}
,

\displaystyle\|T_{Q}(\vec{h})\|_{L^{p}_{W_{\vec{u}}}(\mathbf{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle=\|T_{Q}(\vec{f})\|_{L^{p}_{\mathbf{W}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{H})}\leq[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\|h_{j}\|_{L^{p_{j}}_{\|W_{j}u_{j}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}}(\mathbf{R}^{d})},

as desired.
∎

Remark 4.6
.

In view of the properties of the scalar multilinear Muckenhoupt condition
[
LOP+09
]
, Proposition
4.5
already implies that

\Big[\Big\|\mathbf{W}\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}u_{j}\Big\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{\frac{1}{m}}\Big]_{mp}^{m}\leq[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p}}

as well as that

[\|W^{j}u_{j}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}^{-\frac{1}{m}}]_{mp_{j}^{\prime}}^{m}\leq[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p}},\quad j=1,\ldots,m

for all nonzero
u_{j}\in\mathcal{H}_{j}
,
j=1,\ldots,m
. This already implies that these weights satisfy some reverse Hölder inequalities. However, these do not appear to be sufficient to estimate the mutlilinear convex body sparse operators in Section
6
below, and we need the full result of Theorem
4.4
.

Proposition 4.7
.

Let
\vec{p}\in(0,\infty]^{m}
,
\vec{r}\in(0,\infty)^{m}
and
s\in(\mathbf{R}\backslash\{0\})\cup\{\infty\}
with
\vec{p}\geq\vec{r}
and
\frac{1}{p}\geq\frac{1}{s}
. Let
\vec{W}
be a
m
-tuple of matrix weights with
[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p},(\vec{r},s),\mathrm{op}}<\infty
. Then

[\mathbf{W}]_{p,(r,s),\mathrm{op}}\leq[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p},(\vec{r},s),\mathrm{op}}.

Proof.

Like above, set
\tfrac{1}{q}:=\tfrac{1}{p}-\tfrac{1}{s}
,
\tfrac{1}{t_{j}}:=\tfrac{1}{r_{j}}-\tfrac{1}{p_{j}}
for
j=1,\ldots,m
, and fix a cube
Q\subseteq\mathbf{R}^{d}
. Observe that

\frac{1}{t}=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{1}{t_{j}}=\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{p}.

Thus, by Hölder’s inequality for the exponents
t_{j}
,
j=1,\ldots,m
, we get

\displaystyle\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}\|W_{j}(y)W_{j}(x)^{-1}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}\to\mathcal{H}_{j}}^{t_{j}}\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{\frac{q}{t_{j}}}\mathrm{d}y\Big)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\geq\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\|W_{j}(y)W_{j}(x)^{-1}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}\to\mathcal{H}_{j}}^{t}\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{\frac{q}{t}}\mathrm{d}y\Big)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle=\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}\|\mathbf{W}(y)\mathbf{W}(x)^{-1}\|_{\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{H}}^{t}\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{\frac{q}{t}}\mathrm{d}y\Big)^{\frac{1}{q}},

concluding the proof.
∎

We now prove a matrix weighted version of
[
LMO18
, Lemma 3.2]
. We use the notation similar to the one of
[
Nie20
, Proposition 3.1.6]
.

Proposition 4.8
.

Let
\vec{p}\in(0,\infty]^{m}
,
\vec{r}\in(0,\infty)^{m}
and
s\in(\mathbf{R}\backslash\{0\})\cup\{\infty\}
with
\vec{p}\geq\vec{r}
and
\frac{1}{p}\geq\frac{1}{s}
. Let
\vec{W}
be a
m
-tuple of matrix weights with
[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p},(\vec{r},s),\mathrm{op}}<\infty
. Fix
j\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
. Set

\frac{1}{\sigma_{j}}:=\frac{1}{r_{j}}-\Big(\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{s}\Big)\in\mathbf{R}.

Then

[W_{j}]_{p_{j},(r_{j},\sigma_{j}),\mathrm{op}}\lesssim_{\vec{r},s,\vec{n}}[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p},(\vec{r},s),\mathrm{op}}

and

[W_{j}^{-1}]_{\hat{p}_{j},(r_{j},\sigma_{j}),\mathrm{op}}\lesssim_{\vec{r},s,\vec{n}}[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p},(\vec{r},s),\mathrm{op}},

where
\frac{1}{\hat{p}_{j}}:=\frac{1}{r_{j}}+\frac{1}{\sigma_{j}}-\frac{1}{p_{j}}
.

For the proof we use the following lemma:

Lemma 4.9
.

Let
p\in(0,\infty]
,
r\in(0,\infty)
and
s\in(\mathbf{R}\backslash\{0\})\cup\{\infty\}
with
p\geq r
and
\frac{1}{p}\geq\frac{1}{s}
. Assume that
[W]_{p,(r,s),\mathrm{op}}<\infty
. Set

\frac{1}{\hat{p}}:=\frac{1}{r}+\frac{1}{s}-\frac{1}{p}.

Then, we have
[W^{-1}]_{\hat{p},(r,s),\mathrm{op}}<\infty
and

[W^{-1}]_{\hat{p},(r,s),\mathrm{op}}\eqsim_{r,s,n}\sup_{Q}\|A_{W,Q,\frac{1}{\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{s}}}A_{W^{-1},Q,\frac{1}{\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{p}}}\|_{\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{H}}\eqsim_{r,s,n}[W]_{p,(r,s),\mathrm{op}}.

Proof.

Observing that
\frac{1}{s}\leq\frac{1}{\hat{p}}\leq\frac{1}{r}
and

\frac{1}{\hat{p}}-\frac{1}{s}=\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{p}\quad\text{and}\quad\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{\hat{p}}=\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{s},

this follows from Proposition
3.2
(where we note that
K_{t}\leq 2^{(\frac{1}{r}-1)_{+}}\lesssim_{r}1
and, similarly,
K_{q}\lesssim_{r,s}1
), and the self-adjointness of reducing operators.
∎

Proof of Proposition
4.8
.

Set
\tfrac{1}{q}:=\tfrac{1}{p}-\tfrac{1}{s}
,
\tfrac{1}{t_{j}}:=\tfrac{1}{r_{j}}-\tfrac{1}{p_{j}}
, and
\tfrac{1}{\kappa_{j}}:=\frac{1}{p_{j}}-\tfrac{1}{\sigma_{j}}
for
j=1,\ldots,m
. Then
\kappa_{j}\in(0,\infty]
. Indeed, we have

\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{p}=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\Big(\frac{1}{r_{i}}-\frac{1}{p_{i}}\Big)\geq\frac{1}{r_{j}}-\frac{1}{p_{j}},

which implies that

\frac{1}{p_{j}}-\frac{1}{\sigma_{j}}=\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{s}-\Big(\frac{1}{r_{j}}-\frac{1}{p_{j}}\Big)\geq\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{s}-\Big(\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{p}\Big)=\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{s}\geq 0.

Now fix a cube
Q\subseteq\mathbf{R}^{d}
and
j\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
. By permuting the indices, we may assume without loss of generality that
j=1
. Then we first note that

\|W_{1}(x)^{-1}W_{1}(y)\|_{\mathcal{H}_{1}\to\mathcal{H}_{1}}=\|(W_{1}(x)^{-1}W_{1}(y))\otimes\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{H}_{2}}\ldots\otimes\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{H}_{m}}\|_{\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{H}}.

Since
[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p},(\vec{r},s)}<\infty
, we have that
W_{i}^{-1}
is
t_{i}
-integrable over
Q
. Denoting
A_{i}:=A_{W_{i}^{-1},Q,t_{i}}
for
i=1,\ldots,m
as well as
A^{\otimes}:=\bigotimes_{i=1}^{m}A_{i}
, we write

\displaystyle(W_{1}

\displaystyle(x)^{-1}W_{1}(y))\otimes\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{H}_{2}}\ldots\otimes\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{H}_{m}}

\displaystyle=(W_{1}(x)^{-1}A_{1}^{-1}A_{1}W_{1}(y))\otimes\Big(\bigotimes_{i=2}^{m}(W_{i}(y)^{-1}A_{i}^{-1}A_{i}W_{i}(y))\Big)

\displaystyle=(W_{1}(x)^{-1}A_{1}^{-1})\otimes\Big(\bigotimes_{i=2}^{m}(W_{i}(y)^{-1}A_{i}^{-1})\Big)\Big](A^{\otimes}\mathbf{W}(y)).

Using first the submultiplicativity of the operator norm and then the fact that the operator norm of the tensor product of operators equals the product of the operator norms of the individual operators, we finally get

\displaystyle\|W_{1}(x)^{-1}W_{1}(y)\|_{\mathcal{H}_{1}\to\mathcal{H}_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq\|W_{1}(x)^{-1}A_{1}^{-1}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{1}\to\mathcal{H}_{1}}\Big(\prod_{i=2}^{m}\|W_{i}(y)^{-1}A_{i}^{-1}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{i}\to\mathcal{H}_{i}}\Big)\|A^{\otimes}\mathbf{W}(y)\|_{\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{H}}.

Thus, we deduce

\displaystyle\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}\|W_{1}(x)^{-1}W_{1}(y)\|_{\mathcal{H}_{1}\to\mathcal{H}_{1}}^{t_{1}}\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{\frac{\kappa_{1}}{t_{1}}}\mathrm{d}y\Big)^{\frac{1}{\kappa_{1}}}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}\|W_{1}(x)^{-1}A_{1}^{-1}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{1}\to\mathcal{H}_{1}}^{t_{1}}\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{\frac{1}{t_{1}}}

\displaystyle\times\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}\Big(\prod_{i=2}^{m}\|W_{i}(y)^{-1}A_{i}^{-1}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{i}\to\mathcal{H}_{i}}^{\kappa_{1}}\Big)\|A^{\otimes}\mathbf{W}(y)\|_{\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{H}}^{\kappa_{1}}\mathrm{d}y\Big)^{\frac{1}{\kappa_{1}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{r_{1},n_{1}}\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}\Big(\prod_{i=2}^{m}\|W_{i}(y)^{-1}A_{i}^{-1}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{i}\to\mathcal{H}_{i}}^{\kappa_{1}}\Big)\|A^{\otimes}\mathbf{W}(y)\|_{\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{H}}^{\kappa_{1}}\mathrm{d}y\Big)^{\frac{1}{\kappa_{1}}}.

Now, we observe that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=2}^{m}\frac{1}{t_{i}}+\frac{1}{q}=\frac{1}{t}-\frac{1}{t_{1}}+\frac{1}{q}=\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{s}-\frac{1}{r_{1}}+\frac{1}{p_{1}}=\frac{1}{\kappa_{1}}.

Thus, we apply Hölder’s inequality for the exponents
t_{2},\ldots,t_{m},q
to conclude that

\displaystyle\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}\|W_{1}(x)^{-1}W_{1}(y)\|_{\mathcal{H}_{1}\to\mathcal{H}_{1}}^{t_{1}}\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{\frac{\kappa_{1}}{t_{1}}}\mathrm{d}y\Big)^{\frac{1}{\kappa_{1}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{r_{1},n_{1}}\Big(\prod_{i=2}^{m}\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}\|W_{i}(y)^{-1}A_{i}^{-1}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{i}\to\mathcal{H}_{i}}^{t_{i}}\mathrm{d}y\Big)^{\frac{1}{t_{i}}}\Big)\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}\|A^{\otimes}\mathbf{W}(y)\|_{\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{H}}^{q}\mathrm{d}y\Big)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\vec{r},s,\vec{n}}[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p},(\vec{r},s),\mathrm{op}},

concluding the proof of the first estimate. The second estimate follows immediately from the first one coupled with Lemma
4.9
, proving the result.
∎

We now want to show that the conclusions of Proposition
4.7
and Proposition
4.8
together are actually equivalent to the condition of the weight
m
-tuple belonging to the multilinear matrix Muckenhoupt class.

Theorem 4.10
.

Let
\vec{p}\in(0,\infty]^{m}
,
\vec{r}\in(0,\infty)^{m}
and
s\in(\mathbf{R}\backslash\{0\})\cup\{\infty\}
with
\vec{p}\geq\vec{r}
and
\frac{1}{p}\geq\frac{1}{s}
. Let
\vec{W}
be a
m
-tuple of matrix weights with
[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p},(\vec{r},s),\mathrm{op}}<\infty
. Set

\frac{1}{\sigma_{j}}:=\frac{1}{r_{j}}-\Big(\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{s}\Big),\quad j=1,\ldots,m.

Then, the following are equivalent.

(1)

[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p},(\vec{r},s),\mathrm{op}}<\infty
;

(2)

[W_{j}]_{p_{j},(r_{j},\sigma_{j}),\mathrm{op}}<\infty
for each
j=1,\ldots,m
and
[\mathbf{W}]_{p,(r,s)}<\infty
.

Moreover, in this case we have

\displaystyle\max([W_{1}]_{p_{1},(r_{1},\sigma_{1}),\mathrm{op}},\ldots,[W_{m}]_{p_{m},(r_{m},\sigma_{m}),\mathrm{op}},[\mathbf{W}]_{p,(r,s),\mathrm{op}})\lesssim_{\vec{r},s,\vec{n}}[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p},(\vec{r},s),\mathrm{op}}

and

\displaystyle[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p},(\vec{r},s),\mathrm{op}}\lesssim_{\vec{n},\vec{r},s,m}[\mathbf{W}]_{p,(r,s),\mathrm{op}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}[W]_{p_{j},(r_{j},\sigma_{j}),\mathrm{op}}.

Proof.

The implication
1
\Rightarrow
2
together with the first inequality follow from Proposition
4.7
and Proposition
4.8
. Thus, it remains to show that
2
\Rightarrow
1
together with the second inequality.

Assume that
[W_{j}]_{p_{j},(r_{j},\sigma_{j}),\mathrm{op}}<\infty
for each
j=1,\ldots,m
and
[\mathbf{W}]_{p,(r,s)}<\infty
. We set
\tfrac{1}{\ell}:=m\big(\tfrac{1}{r}-\tfrac{1}{s}\big)
,
\tfrac{1}{q}:=\tfrac{1}{p}-\tfrac{1}{s}
,
\tfrac{1}{t_{j}}:=\tfrac{1}{r_{j}}-\tfrac{1}{p_{j}}
, and
\tfrac{1}{\kappa_{j}}:=\frac{1}{p_{j}}-\tfrac{1}{\sigma_{j}}
for
j=1,\ldots,m
.
Fix a cube
Q\subseteq\mathbf{R}^{d}
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}\|W_{j}(y)W_{j}(x)^{-1}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}\to\mathcal{H}_{j}}^{t_{j}}\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{\frac{q}{t_{j}}}\mathrm{d}y\Big)^{\frac{\ell}{q}}

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}\|W_{j}(y)W_{j}(x)^{-1}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}\to\mathcal{H}_{j}}^{t_{j}}\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{\frac{q}{t_{j}}}\mathrm{d}y\Big)^{\frac{\ell}{q}}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\leq\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}\|W_{j}(y)W_{j}(z)^{-1}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}\to\mathcal{H}_{j}}^{t_{j}}\|W_{j}(z)W_{j}(x)^{-1}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}\to\mathcal{H}_{j}}^{t_{j}}\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{\frac{q}{t_{j}}}\mathrm{d}y\Big)^{\frac{\ell}{q}}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}\|\mathbf{W}(y)\mathbf{W}(z)^{-1}\|_{\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{H}}^{q}\mathrm{d}y\Big)^{\frac{\ell}{q}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}\|W_{j}(z)W_{j}(x)^{-1}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}\to\mathcal{H}_{j}}^{t_{j}}\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{\frac{\ell}{t_{j}}}\mathrm{d}z.

Notice that

\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{1}{\kappa_{j}}+\frac{1}{t}=\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{t}-\frac{1}{r}+m\Big(\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{s}\Big)=\frac{1}{\ell}.

Thus, by applying Hölder’s inequality with the exponents
\tfrac{t}{\ell},\tfrac{\kappa_{1}}{\ell},\ldots,\tfrac{\kappa_{m}}{\ell}
, we deduce

\displaystyle\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}\|W_{j}(y)W_{j}(x)^{-1}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}\to\mathcal{H}_{j}}^{t_{j}}\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{\frac{q}{t_{j}}}\mathrm{d}y\Big)^{\frac{\ell}{q}}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}\|\mathbf{W}(y)\mathbf{W}(z)^{-1}\|_{\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{H}}^{q}\mathrm{d}y\Big)^{\frac{t}{q}}\mathrm{d}z\Big)^{\frac{\ell}{t}}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{j=1}^{m}\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}\|W_{j}(z)W_{j}(x)^{-1}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}\to\mathcal{H}_{j}}^{t_{j}}\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{\frac{\kappa_{j}}{t_{j}}}\mathrm{d}z\Big)^{\frac{\ell}{\kappa_{j}}}

\displaystyle\leq[\mathbf{W}^{-1}]_{\hat{p},(r,s),\mathrm{op}}^{\ell}\prod_{j=1}^{m}[W_{j}]_{p_{j},(r_{j},\sigma_{j}),\mathrm{op}}^{\ell},

where

\frac{1}{\hat{p}}:=\frac{1}{r}+\frac{1}{s}-\frac{1}{p}.

Thus, we have shown that

[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p},(\vec{r},s),\mathrm{op}}\leq[\mathbf{W}^{-1}]_{\hat{p},(r,s),\mathrm{op}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}[W_{j}]_{p_{j},(r_{j},\sigma_{j}),\mathrm{op}}.

An appeal to Lemma
4.9
yields
[\mathbf{W}^{-1}]_{\hat{p},(r,s),\mathrm{op}}\eqsim_{n,r,s}[\mathbf{W}]_{p,(r,s),\mathrm{op}}
, concluding the proof.
∎

Proposition 4.11
.

Let
p\in(0,\infty]
,
r\in(0,\infty)
and
s\in(\mathbf{R}\backslash\{0\})\cup\{\infty\}
with
p\geq r
,
\frac{1}{p}\geq\frac{1}{s}
and
r\neq s
. Assume that
[W]_{p,(r,s),\mathrm{op}}<\infty
. Then, for all
v\in\mathcal{H}\setminus\{0\}
we have

[\|Wv\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{\rho}]^{\frac{1}{\rho}}_{\kappa}\leq[W]_{p,(r,s),\mathrm{op}},

where

\frac{1}{\kappa}:=\frac{\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{s}}{\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{s}}\in[0,1]\quad\text{and}\quad\frac{1}{\rho}:=\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{s}.

Proof.

Set

\frac{1}{t}:=\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{p},\quad\frac{1}{q}:=\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{s}

and observe that

\rho\kappa^{\prime}=t,\quad\rho\kappa=q.

To begin with, we prove the following estimate for measurable functions
f:\mathbf{R}^{d}\to\mathcal{H}
:

(4.1)

\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}\langle\|W(x)f\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{\rho}\rangle_{Q}^{\kappa}\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{\frac{1}{\kappa}}\leq[W]_{p,(r,s),\mathrm{op}}\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}\|W(x)f(x)\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{\rho\kappa}\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{\frac{1}{\kappa}}.

We estimate

\displaystyle\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}\langle\|W(x)f\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{\rho}\rangle_{Q}^{\kappa}\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{\frac{1}{\kappa}}=\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}\|W(x)f(y)\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{\rho}\mathrm{d}y\Big)^{q}\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{\frac{1}{\kappa}}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}\|W(x)W(y)^{-1}\|_{\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{H}}^{\rho}\cdot\|W(y)f(y)\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{\rho}\mathrm{d}y\Big)^{\kappa}\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{\frac{1}{\kappa}}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}\|W(x)W(y)^{-1}\|_{\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{H}}^{\rho\kappa^{\prime}}\mathrm{d}y\Big)^{\frac{\kappa}{\kappa^{\prime}}}\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}\|W(y)f(y)\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{\rho\kappa}\mathrm{d}y\Big)\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{\frac{1}{\kappa}}

\displaystyle\leq[W]_{p,(r,s),\mathrm{op}}^{\rho}\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}\|W(x)f(x)\|_{\mathcal{H}}{\rho\kappa}\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{\frac{1}{\kappa}}.

Next, considering the scalar weight
w:=\|Wv\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{\rho}
, which by Proposition
3.2
is locally
\kappa
-integrable, for any measurable function
f:\mathbf{R}^{d}\to[0,\infty)
we test (
4.1
) on the vector valued function
f^{\frac{1}{\rho}}v
, and we get

\displaystyle\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}\langle\|W(x)f^{\frac{1}{\rho}}v\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{\rho}\rangle_{Q}^{\kappa}\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{\frac{1}{\kappa}}\leq[W]_{p,(r,s),\mathrm{op}}^{\rho}\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}\|W(x)f(x)^{\frac{1}{\rho}}v\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{\rho\kappa}\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{\frac{1}{\kappa}},

that is

\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}(\langle f\rangle_{Q}w(x))^{\kappa}\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{\frac{1}{\kappa}}\leq[W]_{p,(r,s),\mathrm{op}}^{\rho}\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q}}(f(x)w(x))^{\kappa}\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{\frac{1}{\kappa}}.

In other words, we have shown that

(4.2)

\|T_{Q}\|_{L^{\kappa}_{w}\Big(Q,\frac{\mathrm{d}x}{|Q|}\Big)\to L^{\kappa}_{w}\Big(Q,\frac{\mathrm{d}x}{|Q|}\Big)}\leq[W]_{p,(r,s),\mathrm{op}}^{\rho}<\infty.

Since
\kappa\in[1,\infty]
and the scalar weight
w
is
q
-integrable over
Q
, it is a classical fact that

\langle w^{\kappa}\rangle_{Q}^{\frac{1}{\kappa}}\langle w^{-\kappa^{\prime}}\rangle_{Q}^{\frac{1}{\kappa^{\prime}}}\leq\|T_{Q}\|_{L^{\kappa}_{w}\Big(Q,\frac{\mathrm{d}x}{|Q|}\Big)\to L^{\kappa}_{w}\Big(Q,\frac{\mathrm{d}x}{|Q|}\Big)},

yielding the required result.
∎

Remark 4.12
.

Referring to the proof of Proposition
4.11
, we note that

w^{-1}\leq\frac{\|W^{-1}v\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{\rho}}{\|u\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2\rho}}\quad\text{a.e.\penalty\ on }\mathbf{R}^{d}.

Since
W^{-1}
is locally
t
-integrable by Proposition
3.2
, we have that
w^{-1}
is locally
\kappa^{\prime}
-integrable.

Corollary 4.13
.

Let
\vec{p}\in(0,\infty]^{m}
,
\vec{r}\in(0,\infty)^{m}
and
s\in(\mathbf{R}\backslash\{0\})\cup\{\infty\}
with
\vec{p}\geq\vec{r}
,
\frac{1}{p}\geq\frac{1}{s}
. and
r\neq s
. Let
\vec{W}
be a
m
-tuple of matrix weights with
[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p},(\vec{r},s),\mathrm{op}}<\infty
. Set

\frac{1}{q}:=\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{s},\quad\frac{1}{\kappa}:=\frac{\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{s}}{\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{s}},\quad\frac{1}{\rho}:=\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{s},

and

\frac{1}{t_{j}}:=\frac{1}{r_{j}}-\frac{1}{p_{j}},\quad\lambda_{j}:=t_{j}\Big(\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{s}\Big),\quad j=1,\ldots,m.

Then we have

(4.3)

\sup_{v\in\mathcal{H}\setminus\{0\}}[\|\mathbf{W}v\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{\rho}]^{\frac{1}{\rho}}_{\kappa}\leq[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p},(\vec{r},s),\mathrm{op}},

and

(4.4)

\sup_{v\in\mathcal{H}_{j}\setminus\{0\}}[\|W_{j}^{-1}v\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}^{\rho}]^{\frac{1}{\rho}}_{\lambda_{j}}\lesssim_{\vec{r},s,\vec{n}}[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p},(\vec{r},s),\mathrm{op}},\quad\forall j=1,\ldots,m.

In particular, we have

(4.5)

[\mathbf{W}]_{\mathrm{FW}_{q}}:=\sup_{v\in\mathcal{H}\setminus\{0\}}[\|\mathbf{W}v\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{q}]_{\mathrm{FW}}^{\frac{1}{q}}\lesssim_{r,s,p}[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p},(\vec{r},s),\mathrm{op}}\quad\text{if }p\neq s

and

(4.6)

[W_{j}^{-1}]_{\mathrm{FW}_{t_{j}}}:=\sup_{v\in\mathcal{H}_{j}\setminus\{0\}}[\|W_{j}^{-1}v\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}^{t_{j}}]_{\mathrm{FW}}^{\frac{1}{t_{j}}}\lesssim_{\vec{r},s,\vec{n},p_{j}}[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p},(\vec{r},s),\mathrm{op}}\quad\text{ if $p_{j}\neq r_{j}$}

for all
j=1,\ldots,m
.

Proof.

Estimate (
4.3
) follows immediately from Proposition
4.7
combined with Proposition
4.11
. Estimate (
4.4
) follows immediately from Proposition
4.8
combined with Proposition
4.11
(since in the notation of Proposition
4.8
we have
\sigma_{j}\neq r_{j}
for each
j=1,\ldots,m
).

Finally, since
\rho\kappa=q
and
\lambda_{j}\rho=t_{j}
for all
j=1,\ldots,m
, (
4.5
) and (
4.6
) follow from (
4.3
), respectively (
4.4
), through the fact that if
\alpha\in[1,\infty)
and
w
is a scalar weight, then

[w^{\alpha}]^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}_{\text{FW}}\lesssim_{\alpha}[w]_{\alpha},

see, e.g.,
[
HP13
]
. The result follows.
∎

Remark 4.14
.

If one cares about dependence on
\vec{p}
, one can use the fact that if
\alpha\leq\beta
and
w
is a scalar weight, then

\Big[w^{-\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\alpha}-\frac{1}{\beta}}}\Big]^{\frac{1}{\beta}}_{\mathrm{FW}}\leq e^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}[w^{\alpha}]_{\frac{\beta}{\alpha}}^{\frac{\frac{1}{\alpha}\frac{1}{\beta}}{\frac{1}{\beta}-\frac{1}{\alpha}}},

see
[
Nie20
, Proposition 3.3.3]
. If one applies this in the final estimate above respectively with
\alpha=\rho
,
\tfrac{1}{\beta}=\tfrac{1}{r}-\tfrac{1}{p}
,
w=\|Wv\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{-\rho}
, and
\alpha=\rho
,
\beta=t_{j}
,
w=\|W_{j}^{-1}v\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}
, we obtain

\sup_{v\in\mathcal{H}\setminus\{0\}}[\|\mathbf{W}v\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{q}]_{\mathrm{FW}}^{\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{p}}\lesssim_{r,s}[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p},(\vec{r},s),\mathrm{op}}^{\frac{\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{p}}{\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{s}}}\quad\text{if }p\neq s

and

[W_{j}^{-1}]_{\mathrm{FW}_{\frac{1}{\frac{1}{r_{j}}-\frac{1}{p_{j}}}}}^{\frac{\frac{1}{p_{j}}}{\frac{1}{r_{j}}-\frac{1}{p_{j}}}}=\sup_{v\in\mathcal{H}_{j}\setminus\{0\}}[\|W_{j}^{-1}v\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}^{t_{j}}]_{\mathrm{FW}}^{\frac{1}{p_{j}}}\lesssim_{\vec{r},s,\vec{n}}[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p},(\vec{r},s),\mathrm{op}}^{\frac{t_{j}}{p_{j}}}\quad\text{ if $p_{j}\neq r_{j}$}

for all
j=1,\ldots,m
. Setting
\vec{r}=(1,\ldots,1)
,
s=\infty
, this yields

\sup_{v\in\mathcal{H}\setminus\{0\}}[\|\mathbf{W}v\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{p}]_{\mathrm{FW}}^{m-\frac{1}{p}}\lesssim_{m}[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p},\mathrm{op}}^{pm-1}\quad\text{if }p<\infty

and

\sup_{v\in\mathcal{H}_{j}\setminus\{0\}}[\|W_{j}^{-1}v\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}^{p^{\prime}_{j}}]_{\mathrm{FW}}^{\frac{1}{p_{j}}}\lesssim_{\vec{r},s,\vec{n}}[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p},\mathrm{op}}^{\frac{p^{\prime}_{j}}{p_{j}}}\quad\text{ if $p_{j}>1$}

for all
j=1,\ldots,m
. As these last terms are exactly the ones that appears in Theorem
B
(and Theorem
5.4
below), the dependence on
\vec{p}
can be determined more precisely this way.

Proof of Theorem
4.4
.

The result follows from Corollary
4.13
applied with
\vec{r}=(1,\ldots,1)
and
s=\infty
.
∎

5.
The tensor product maximal operator

Given
\vec{F}=(F_{1},\ldots,F_{m})
with
F_{j}\in L^{1}_{\text{loc}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H}_{j}))
and a collection of cubes
\mathcal{P}
we define

M^{\mathcal{K}}_{\mathcal{P}}\vec{F}(x):=\mathcal{K}\Big(\bigcup_{Q\in\mathcal{P}}\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}\langle F_{j}\rangle_{Q}\ind_{Q}(x)\Big)

and

M_{\mathcal{P}}\vec{F}(x):=\overline{\bigcup_{Q\in\mathcal{P}}\mathcal{K}\Big(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}\langle F_{j}\rangle_{Q}\Big)\ind_{Q}(x)}

Moreover, we drop the index
\mathcal{P}
when the collection consists of all cubes.

5.1.
Weak type bounds

We define
L^{p}_{\mathbf{W}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{H})_{\text{weak}}
by

\|F\|_{L^{p}_{\mathbf{W}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{H})_{\text{weak}}}:=\sup_{u\in\mathcal{H}}\|\ind_{\{x\in\mathbf{R}^{d}:u\in F(x)\}}u\|_{L^{p}_{\mathbf{W}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{H})}.

As
\ind_{\{x\in\mathbf{R}^{d}:u\in F(x)\}}u\in S^{0}(\mathbf{R}^{d};F)
, any
F\in L^{p}_{\mathbf{W}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{H})
belongs to
L^{p}_{\mathbf{W}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{H})_{\text{weak}}
with

\|F\|_{L^{p}_{\mathbf{W}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{H})_{\text{weak}}}\leq\|F\|_{L^{p}_{\mathbf{W}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{H})}.

The following result shows that the weak-type boundedness of
M
characterizes the multilinear matrix Muckenhoupt condition.

Proposition 5.1
.

Let
\vec{p}\in[1,\infty]^{m}
and let
\vec{W}
be matrix weights. Then the following are equivalent:

(1)

\vec{W}\in A_{\vec{p}}
;

(2)

M:L^{\vec{p}}_{\vec{W}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{K}(\vec{\mathcal{H}}))\to L^{p}_{\mathbf{W}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H}))_{\emph{weak}}
.

Moreover, in this case we have

[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p}}\eqsim_{d,n,m}\|M\|_{L^{\vec{p}}_{\vec{W}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{K}(\vec{\mathcal{H}}))\to L^{p}_{\mathbf{W}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H}))_{\emph{weak}}}.

For the proof, we require the following lemma:

Lemma 5.2
.

Let
\vec{p}\in[1,\infty]^{m}
,
\vec{W}\in A_{\vec{p}}
,
\vec{F}\in L^{\vec{p}}_{\vec{W}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{K}(\vec{\mathcal{H}}))
, and let
\mathcal{P}
be a pairwise disjoint collection of cubes in a dyadic grid
\mathcal{D}
. Then

\Big\|\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{P}}\mathcal{K}\Big(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}\langle F_{j}\rangle_{Q}\Big)\ind_{Q}\Big\|_{L^{p}_{\mathbf{W}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H}))}\lesssim_{n,m}[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\|F_{j}\|_{L^{p_{j}}_{W_{j}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H}_{j}))}.

Proof.

We set
T_{\mathcal{P}}\vec{F}:=\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{P}_{k}}\mathcal{K}\big(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}\langle F_{j}\rangle_{Q}\big)\ind_{Q}
and let
g\in S^{0}(\mathbf{R}^{d};T_{\mathcal{P}}\vec{F})
. Then
g=\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{P}}g_{Q}
, where
g_{Q}\in S^{0}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{K}(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}\langle F_{j}\rangle_{Q}\ind_{Q}))
. By the John ellipsoid theorem, there is an
A_{Q}\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{H})
such that

(5.1)

A_{Q}\overline{B}\subseteq\mathcal{K}\Big(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}\langle F_{j}\rangle_{Q}\ind_{Q}\Big)\subseteq n^{\frac{1}{2}}A_{Q}\overline{B},

where
\overline{B}
is the closed unit ball in
\mathcal{H}=\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}\mathcal{H}_{j}
. Choosing an orthonormal basis
(e_{k})_{k=1}^{n_{j}}
for each
\mathcal{H}_{j}
, for
\vec{k}=(k_{1},\ldots,k_{m})
,
k_{j}\in\{1,\ldots,n_{j}\}
, we write
e_{\vec{k}}:=\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}e_{k_{j}}
, and set
v_{\vec{k}}^{Q}:=A_{Q}e_{\vec{k}}
. By the first inclusion in (
5.1
) and Carathéodory’s theorem on convex hulls, we have

v_{\vec{k}}^{Q}=\sum_{i=1}^{2n+1}\theta_{i,\vec{k}}^{Q}\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}\langle f_{\vec{k},i,j}^{Q}\rangle_{Q},

with
\theta_{i,\vec{k}}^{Q}\geq 0
,
\sum_{i=1}^{2n+1}\theta_{i,\vec{k}}^{Q}=1
, and
f_{\vec{k},i,j}^{Q}\in S^{0}(\mathbf{R}^{d};F_{j}\ind_{Q})
. Now, since
g_{Q}(x)\in n^{\frac{1}{2}}A_{Q}\overline{B}
by the second inclusion in (
5.1
), it is of the form

g_{Q}(x)=\sum_{\vec{k}}h_{\vec{k}}^{Q}(x)v_{\vec{k}}^{Q}=\sum_{\vec{k}}h_{\vec{k}}^{Q}(x)\sum_{i=1}^{2n+1}\theta_{i,\vec{k}}^{Q}\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}\langle f_{\vec{k},i,j}^{Q}\rangle_{Q}\ind_{Q}(x),

for
h^{Q}_{\vec{k}}
satisfying
\Big(\sum_{\vec{k}}|h^{Q}_{\vec{k}}(x)|^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq n^{\frac{1}{2}}
. Now, setting
h_{\vec{k}}:=\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{P}}h^{Q}_{\vec{k}}\ind_{Q}
, it follows from the quasi-triangle inequality that

\displaystyle\|g\|_{L^{p}_{\mathbf{W}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\vec{\mathcal{H}})}

\displaystyle=\Big(\int_{\mathbf{R}^{d}}\Big|\sum_{\vec{k}}h_{\vec{k}}(x)\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{P}}\sum_{i=1}^{2n+1}\theta_{i,\vec{k}}^{Q}\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}W_{j}(x)\langle f_{\vec{k},i,j}^{Q}\rangle_{Q}\ind_{Q}\Big|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{(n-1)(\frac{1}{p}-1)_{+}}n^{\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{\vec{k}}\Big(\int_{\mathbf{R}^{d}}\Big|\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{P}}\sum_{i=1}^{2n+1}\theta_{i,\vec{k}}^{Q}\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}W_{j}(x)\langle f_{\vec{k},i,j}^{Q}\rangle_{Q}\ind_{Q}\Big|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{n,m}\sum_{\vec{k}}\Big(\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{P}}\int_{Q}\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{2n+1}\theta_{i,\vec{k}}^{Q}\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}W_{j}(x)\langle f_{\vec{k},i,j}^{Q}\rangle_{Q}\Big|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{n,m}\sum_{\vec{k}}\Big(\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{P}}\sum_{i=1}^{2n+1}(\theta_{i,\vec{k}}^{Q})^{p}\|T_{Q}\vec{f}_{\vec{k},i}^{Q}\|_{L^{p}_{\mathbf{W}}(\mathbf{R}^{d})}^{p}\Big)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p}}\sum_{\vec{k}}\Big(\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{P}}\sum_{i=1}^{2n+1}(\theta_{i,\vec{k}}^{Q})^{p}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\Big(\int_{Q}\!|W_{j}F_{j}|^{p_{j}}\,\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{\frac{p}{p_{j}}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{n}(2n+1)^{\frac{1}{p}}[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p}}\Big(\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{P}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\Big(\int_{Q}\!|W_{j}F_{j}|^{p_{j}}\,\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{\frac{p}{p_{j}}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{n,m}[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\Big(\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{P}}\int_{Q}\!|W_{j}F_{j}|^{p_{j}}\,\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{\frac{1}{p_{j}}}\leq[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\|F_{j}\|_{L^{p_{j}}_{W_{j}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H}_{j}))}.

Taking a supremum over all
g\in S^{0}(\mathbf{R}^{d};T_{\mathcal{P}}\vec{F})
, the assertion follows.
∎

Proof of Proposition
5.1
.

For
2
\Rightarrow
1
, note that
T_{Q}\vec{f}
is a selection of
M(\mathcal{K}(\vec{f}))
. Hence,

\displaystyle\|T_{Q}\vec{f}\|_{L^{p}_{\mathbf{W}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{H})}

\displaystyle=\|T_{Q}\vec{f}\|_{L^{p}_{\mathbf{W}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{H})_{\text{weak}}}\leq\|M(\mathcal{K}(\vec{f}))\|_{L^{p}_{\mathbf{W}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H}))_{\text{weak}}}

\displaystyle\leq\|M\|_{L^{\vec{p}}_{\vec{W}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{K}(\vec{\mathcal{H}}))\to L^{p}_{\mathbf{W}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H}))_{\emph{weak}}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\|\mathcal{K}(f_{j})\|_{L^{p_{j}}_{W_{j}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H}_{j}))}

\displaystyle=\|M\|_{L^{\vec{p}}_{\vec{W}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{K}(\vec{\mathcal{H}}))\to L^{p}_{\mathbf{W}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H}))_{\emph{weak}}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\|f_{j}\|_{L^{p_{j}}_{W_{j}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{H}_{j})}.

This proves that
\vec{W}\in A_{\vec{p}}
with

[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p}}\leq\|M\|_{L^{\vec{p}}_{\vec{W}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\vec{\mathcal{H}})\to L^{p}_{\mathbf{W}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{H})_{\text{weak}}}.

For
1
\Rightarrow
2
, by a
3^{d}
-lattice reduction and the monotone convergence property it suffices to bound
M_{\mathcal{F}}
uniformly for finite collections of cubes
\mathcal{F}
contained in a dyadic grid
\mathcal{D}
.

Let
u\in\mathcal{H}
. If
u\in M_{\mathcal{F}}\vec{F}(x)
, there is a
Q\in\mathcal{F}
with
x\in Q
for which
u\in\mathcal{K}\Big(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}\langle F_{j}\rangle_{Q}\Big)
. Denote the maximal cubes
Q\in\mathcal{F}
for which
u\in\mathcal{K}\Big(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}\langle F_{j}\rangle_{Q}\Big)
by
\mathcal{P}
. Then

\ind_{\{x\in\mathbf{R}^{d}:u\in M_{\mathcal{F}}\vec{F}(x)\}}u\in\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{P}}\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}\langle F_{j}\rangle_{Q}\ind_{Q}

a.e., so that by Lemma
5.2
we have

\displaystyle\|\ind_{\{x\in\mathbf{R}^{d}:u\in M_{\mathcal{F}}\vec{F}(x)\}}u\|_{L^{p}_{\mathbf{W}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{H})}

\displaystyle\leq\Big\|\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{P}}\mathcal{K}\Big(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}\langle F_{j}\rangle_{Q}\Big)\ind_{Q}\Big\|_{L^{p}_{\mathbf{W}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H}))}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{n,m}[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\|F_{j}\|_{L^{p_{j}}_{W_{j}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H}_{j}))}.

The result follows.
∎

5.2.
Strong type upper bounds

Next, we prove a strong-type bound for
M^{\mathcal{K}}
.

Theorem 5.3
.

Let
\vec{p}\in(1,\infty]^{m}
and
\vec{W}\in A_{\vec{p}}
. Then

M^{\mathcal{K}}:L^{\vec{p}}_{\vec{W}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{K}(\vec{\mathcal{H}}))\to L^{p}_{\mathbf{W}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H}))

with

\displaystyle\|M^{\mathcal{K}}\|_{L^{\vec{p}}_{\vec{W}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{K}(\vec{\mathcal{H}}))\to L^{p}_{\mathbf{W}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H}))}.

\displaystyle\lesssim_{d,m,\vec{n},\vec{p}}[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}[W_{j}^{-1}]_{\mathrm{FW}_{p_{j}^{\prime}}}^{\frac{p_{j}^{\prime}}{p_{j}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{d,m,\vec{n},\vec{p}}[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p}}^{1+\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{p_{j}^{\prime}}{p_{j}}}.

We begin with a couple of standard reductions. Using Proposition
2.7
, for any
\vec{F}\in L^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{K}(\vec{\mathcal{H}}))
we have

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{W}(x)\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{K}}(\vec{F})(x)\|_{\mathcal{H}}=\Big\|\mathbf{W}(x)\mathcal{K}\Big(\bigcup_{Q}\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}\langle F_{j}\rangle_{Q}\ind_{Q}(x)\Big)\Big\|_{\mathcal{H}}=\Big\|\bigcup_{Q}\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}\langle W_{j}(x)F_{j}\rangle_{Q}\ind_{Q}(x)\Big\|_{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle=\sup_{Q}\Big\|\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}\langle W_{j}(x)F_{j}\rangle_{Q}\Big\|_{\mathcal{H}}\ind_{Q}(x)=\sup_{Q}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\Big\|\langle W_{j}(x)F_{j}\rangle_{Q}\Big\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}\ind_{Q}(x)

\displaystyle\eqsim_{n}\sup_{Q}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\langle\|W_{j}(x)F_{j}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}\rangle_{Q}\ind_{Q}(x):=\mathcal{M}_{\vec{W}}(\vec{F})(x).

Thus, we only have to find
L^{\vec{p}}_{\vec{W}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H}))\to L^{p}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
bounds for
\mathcal{M}_{\vec{W}}
.

We will actually prove a more general result. Let
\vec{p}\in(0,\infty]^{m}
and
\vec{r}\in(0,\infty)^{m}
with
\vec{p}\geq\vec{r}
,
p\neq\infty
and
p_{j}\neq r_{j}
for all
j=1,\ldots,m
. Let
\vec{W}
be a
m
-tuple of matrix weights with
[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p},(\vec{r},\infty),\mathrm{op}}<\infty
. For
\vec{F}\in L^{\vec{r}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{K}(\vec{\mathcal{H}}))
we define

\mathcal{M}_{\vec{W},\vec{r}}(\vec{F})(x):=\sup_{Q}\Big(\prod_{j=1}^{m}\langle\|W_{j}(x)F_{j}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}^{r_{j}}\rangle_{Q}^{\frac{1}{r_{j}}}\Big)\ind_{Q}(x),

where the supremum is taken over all cubes
Q\subseteq\mathbf{R}^{d}
. We will deduce
L^{\vec{p}}_{\vec{W}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{K}(\vec{\mathcal{H}}))\to L^{p}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
bounds for
\mathcal{M}_{\vec{W},\vec{r}}
in terms of
[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p},(\vec{r},\infty),\mathrm{op}}
.

Theorem 5.4
.

Let
\vec{p}\in(0,\infty]^{m}
and
\vec{r}\in(0,\infty)^{m}
with
\vec{p}>\vec{r}
. Let
\vec{W}
be a
m
-tuple of matrix weights with
[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p},(\vec{r},\infty),\mathrm{op}}<\infty
. We have

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{M}_{\vec{W},\vec{r}}\|_{L^{\vec{p}}_{\vec{W}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{K}(\vec{\mathcal{H}}))\to L^{p}(\mathbf{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{d,m,\vec{n},\vec{p},\vec{r}}[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p},(\vec{r},\infty),\mathrm{op}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}[W_{j}^{-1}]_{\mathrm{FW}_{\frac{1}{\frac{1}{r_{j}}-\frac{1}{p_{j}}}}}^{\frac{\frac{1}{p_{j}}}{\frac{1}{r_{j}}-\frac{1}{p_{j}}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{d,m,\vec{n},\vec{r}}[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p},(\vec{r},\infty),\mathrm{op}}^{1+\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{\frac{1}{p_{j}}}{\frac{1}{r_{j}}-\frac{1}{p_{j}}}}.

The second inequality follows from the first through Corollary
4.13
(and Remark
4.14
), so we need only prove the first one. Moreover, we note that by setting
\vec{r}=(1,\ldots,1)
, Theorem
5.3
follows from this one.

The proof of Theorem
5.4
follows the same strategy as in
[
Gol03
, Theorem 3.2]
. We first need to bound two auxiliary operators. The first one is the multilinear counterpart of “Goldberg’s auxiliary maximal operator” from
[
Gol03
]
and is of interest in its own. Namely, for
\vec{F}\in L^{\vec{r}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{K}(\vec{\mathcal{H}}))
we set

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\vec{W},\vec{r}}(\vec{F})(x):=\sup_{Q}\Big(\prod_{j=1}^{m}\langle\|(A_{W_{j}^{-1},Q,t_{j}})^{-1}F_{j}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}^{r_{j}}\rangle_{Q}^{\frac{1}{r_{j}}}\Big)\ind_{Q}(x),

where the supremum is taken over all cubes
Q\subseteq\mathbf{R}^{d}
. If
\vec{r}=(1,\ldots,1)
then we write
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\vec{W}}:=\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\vec{W},\vec{r}}
.

Proposition 5.5
.

Let
\vec{p}\in(0,\infty]^{m}
and
\vec{r}\in(0,\infty)^{m}
with
\vec{p}>\vec{r}
. Let
\vec{W}
be a
m
-tuple of matrix weights with
[W_{j}^{-1}]_{\mathrm{FW}_{\frac{1}{\frac{1}{r_{j}}-\frac{1}{p_{j}}}}}<\infty
for all
j=1,\ldots,m
. We have

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\vec{W},\vec{r}}\|_{L^{\vec{p}}_{\vec{W}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{K}(\vec{\mathcal{H}}))\to L^{p}(\mathbf{R}^{d})}
