\displaystyle\lesssim_{d,\vec{n},\vec{p},\vec{r}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}[W_{j}^{-1}]_{\mathrm{FW}_{\frac{1}{\frac{1}{r_{j}}-\frac{1}{p_{j}}}}}^{\frac{\frac{1}{p_{j}}}{\frac{1}{r_{j}}-\frac{1}{p_{j}}}}.

Proof.

We adapt the argument of
[
IKP17
, p. 252]
. Set

\frac{1}{\vec{t}}:=\frac{1}{\vec{r}}-\frac{1}{\vec{p}},\quad\vec{\kappa}:=\frac{\vec{p}}{\vec{r}}.

We note that
\vec{r}\leq\vec{t}
. For each
j=1,\ldots,m
let
\tau_{j}\in(1,\infty)
with

\tau_{j}^{\prime}=2^{d+1}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}A\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{H}_{j})\\
A\text{ invertible}\end{subarray}}[\|W_{j}A\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}\to\mathcal{H}_{j}}^{t_{j}}]_{\mathrm{FW}},

where as a consequence of Lemma
2.6
we observe that

\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}A\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{H}_{j})\\
A\text{ invertible}\end{subarray}}[\|W_{j}A\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}\to\mathcal{H}_{j}}^{t_{j}}]^{\frac{1}{t_{j}}}_{\mathrm{FW}}\lesssim_{n}[W_{j}^{-1}]_{\mathrm{FW}_{t_{j}}}<\infty.

Then, using in each factor Hölder’s inequality for the exponents
\frac{\tau_{j}t_{j}}{r_{j}}
,
\Big(\frac{\tau_{j}t_{j}}{r_{j}}\Big)^{\prime}
(where we note that
t_{j}\geq r_{j}
), the sharp Reverse Hölder inequality
[
HPR12
, Theorem 2.3]
, and Lemma
2.6
, we estimate

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\vec{W},\vec{r}}(\vec{F})(x)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{Q}\Big(\prod_{j=1}^{m}\langle\|(A_{W_{j}^{-1},Q,t_{j}})^{-1}W_{j}^{-1}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}\to\mathcal{H}_{j}}^{\tau_{j}t_{j}}\rangle_{Q}^{\frac{1}{\tau_{j}t_{j}}}\langle\|W_{j}F_{j}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}^{r_{j}\Big(\frac{\tau_{j}t_{j}}{r_{j}}\Big)^{\prime}}\rangle_{Q}^{\frac{1}{r_{j}\Big(\frac{\tau_{j}t_{j}}{r_{j}}\Big)^{\prime}}}\Big)\ind_{Q}(x)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\vec{r}}\sup_{Q}\Big(\prod_{j=1}^{m}\langle\|W_{j}^{-1}(A_{W_{j}^{-1},Q,t_{j}})^{-1}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}\to\mathcal{H}_{j}}^{t_{j}}\rangle_{Q}^{\frac{1}{t_{j}}}\langle\|W_{j}F_{j}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}^{r_{j}\Big(\frac{\tau_{j}t_{j}}{r_{j}}\Big)^{\prime}}\rangle_{Q}^{\frac{1}{r_{j}\Big(\frac{\tau_{j}t_{j}}{r_{j}}\Big)^{\prime}}}\Big)\ind_{Q}(x)

\displaystyle\eqsim_{\vec{n},\vec{r}}\sup_{Q}\Big(\prod_{j=1}^{m}\langle\|W_{j}F_{j}\|^{r_{j}\Big(\frac{\tau_{j}t_{j}}{r_{j}}\Big)^{\prime}}_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}\rangle_{Q}^{\frac{1}{r_{j}\Big(\frac{\tau_{j}t_{j}}{r_{j}}\Big)^{\prime}}}\Big)\ind_{Q}(x).

Set

w_{j}:=\frac{p_{j}}{r_{j}\Big(\frac{\tau_{j}t_{j}}{r_{j}}\Big)^{\prime}},\quad j=1,\ldots,m.

We observe that

\displaystyle\frac{\tau_{j}t_{j}}{r_{j}}>\frac{t_{j}}{r_{j}}=\kappa_{j}^{\prime},

therefore
w_{j}=\frac{\kappa_{j}}{\Big(\frac{\tau_{j}t_{j}}{r_{j}}\Big)^{\prime}}>1
. Thus, applying again Hölder’s inequality and then the classical strong type estimate for the Hardy–Littlewood maximal function
M
on
\mathbf{R}^{d}
we obtain

\displaystyle\Big(\int_{\mathbf{R}^{d}}\widetilde{M}_{\vec{W},\vec{r}}(\vec{F})(x)^{p}\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{\frac{1}{p}}\lesssim_{\vec{n},\vec{r}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\Big(\int_{\mathbf{R}^{d}}M(\|W_{j}F_{j}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}^{r_{j}\Big(\frac{\tau_{j}t_{j}}{r_{j}}\Big)^{\prime}})(x)^{\frac{p_{j}}{r_{j}\Big(\frac{\tau_{j}r_{j}}{r_{j}}\Big)^{\prime}}}\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{\frac{1}{p_{j}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{d,\vec{p},\vec{r}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}(w_{j}^{\prime})^{\frac{1}{p_{j}}}\Big(\int_{\mathbf{R}^{d}}\|W_{j}(x)F_{j}(x)\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}^{p_{j}}\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{\frac{1}{p_{j}}}.

Now notice that

\displaystyle w_{j}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\frac{p_{j}}{p_{j}-r_{j}\Big(\frac{\tau_{j}t_{j}}{r_{j}}\Big)^{\prime}}=\frac{p_{j}}{p_{j}-\frac{r_{j}\tau_{j}t_{j}}{\tau_{j}t_{j}-r_{j}}}=\frac{p_{j}(\tau_{j}t_{j}-r_{j})}{p_{j}(\tau_{j}t_{j}-r_{j})-r_{j}\tau_{j}t_{j}}

\displaystyle=\frac{p_{j}(\tau_{j}t_{j}-r_{j})}{\tau_{j}t_{j}(p_{j}-r_{j})-p_{j}r_{j}}=\frac{\frac{\tau_{j}t_{j}}{r_{j}}-1}{\tau_{j}t_{j}\Big(\frac{1}{r_{j}}-\frac{1}{p_{j}}\Big)-1}=\frac{\frac{\tau_{j}t_{j}}{r_{j}}-1}{\tau_{j}-1}\lesssim_{p_{j},r_{j}}\tau_{j}^{\prime},

therefore

\displaystyle(w_{j}^{\prime})^{\frac{1}{p_{j}}}\lesssim_{p_{j},r_{j},s}(\tau_{j}^{\prime})^{\frac{1}{p_{j}}}\lesssim_{d,n,p_{j},r_{j}}[W_{j}^{-1}]_{\mathrm{FW}_{t_{j}}}^{\frac{t_{j}}{p_{j}}}.

It now remains to observe that
\frac{t_{j}}{p_{j}}=\frac{\frac{1}{p_{j}}}{\frac{1}{r_{j}}-\frac{1}{p_{j}}}
.
∎

Lemma 5.6
.

Suppose
p<\infty
. Let
\mathcal{F}
be a finite family of cubes contained in a dyadic grid
\mathcal{D}
. For all
Q\in\mathcal{F}
, we consider the auxiliary operator

N_{Q,\mathcal{F}}(x):=\sup_{R\in\mathcal{F}(Q)}\Big(\prod_{j=1}^{m}\|W_{j}(x)A_{W_{j}^{-1},R,t_{j}}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}\to\mathcal{H}_{j}}\Big)\ind_{R}(x).

Then

\int_{Q}N_{Q,\mathcal{F}}(x)^{p}\mathrm{d}x\lesssim_{\vec{n},\vec{r},\vec{p}}[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p},(\vec{r},\infty),\mathrm{op}}^{p}|Q|

for all
Q\in\mathcal{F}
.

Proof.

We adapt the proof of
[
Gol03
, Lemma 3.3]
. Since
[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p},(\vec{r},\infty),\mathrm{op}}<\infty
, we have that
\mathbf{W}
is
p
-integrable over
Q
. Since
\mathcal{F}
is finite, a crude estimate shows that there is a best constant
0<B<\infty
such that

\displaystyle\int_{Q}N_{Q,\mathcal{F}}(x)^{p}\mathrm{d}x\leq B|Q|,

for all
Q\in\mathcal{F}
. We show that
B\lesssim_{\vec{n},\vec{r},\vec{p}}[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p},(\vec{r},\infty),\mathrm{op}}^{p}
.

Let
0<A<\infty
be a large constant to be specified later. Fix
Q\in\mathcal{F}
. Denote by
\mathcal{S}
the set of maximal cubes
R
in
\mathcal{F}(Q)
satisfying

\prod_{j=1}^{m}\|(A_{W_{j}^{-1},Q,t_{j}})^{-1}A_{W_{j}^{-1},R,t_{j}}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}\to\mathcal{H}_{j}}>A.

It is clear that

N_{Q,\mathcal{F}}(x)\leq A\prod_{j=1}^{m}\|W_{j}(x)A_{W_{j}^{-1},Q,t_{j}}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}\to\mathcal{H}_{j}},\quad\forall x\in Q\setminus(\bigcup\mathcal{S}),

therefore, using Lemma
2.6
,

\displaystyle\int_{Q\setminus(\bigcup\mathcal{S})}N_{Q,\mathcal{F}}(x)^{p}\mathrm{d}x\leq A^{p}\int_{Q}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\|W_{j}(x)A_{W_{j}^{-1},Q,t_{j}}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}\to\mathcal{H}_{j}}^{p}\mathrm{d}x\leq c^{p}A^{p}[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p},(\vec{r},\infty),\mathrm{op}}^{p}|Q|,

where the constant
c>0
depends only on
\vec{n},\vec{r}
.

We now claim that

\sum_{R\in\mathcal{S}}|R|<\frac{1}{2}|Q|

if
A
is sufficiently large. Indeed, for each
R\in\mathcal{S}
we estimate, using Lemma
2.6
,

\displaystyle A^{t}\leq\prod_{j=1}^{m}\|(A_{W_{j}^{-1},Q,t_{j}})^{-1}A_{W_{j}^{-1},R,t_{j}}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}\to\mathcal{H}_{j}}^{t}

\displaystyle\eqsim_{\vec{n},\vec{p},\vec{r},m}\frac{1}{|R|}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\Big(\int_{R}\|W_{j}(x)^{-1}(A_{W_{j}^{-1},Q,t_{j}})^{-1}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}\to\mathcal{H}_{j}}^{t_{j}}\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{\frac{t}{t_{j}}},

so by applying Hölder’s inequality (for sums) we obtain

\displaystyle A^{t}\sum_{R\in\mathcal{S}}|R|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\vec{n},\vec{p},\vec{r},m}\sum_{R\in\mathcal{S}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\Big(\int_{R}\|W_{j}(x)^{-1}(A_{W_{j}^{-1},Q,t_{j}})^{-1}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}\to\mathcal{H}_{j}}^{t_{j}}\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{\frac{t}{t_{j}}}

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{j=1}^{m}\Big(\sum_{R\in\mathcal{S}}\int_{R}\|W_{j}(x)^{-1}(A_{W_{j}^{-1},Q,t_{j}})^{-1}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}\to\mathcal{H}_{j}}^{t_{j}}\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{\frac{t}{t_{j}}}

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{j=1}^{m}\Big(\int_{Q}\|W_{j}(x)^{-1}(A_{W_{j}^{-1},Q,t_{j}})^{-1}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}\to\mathcal{H}_{j}}^{t_{j}}\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{\frac{t}{t_{j}}}\eqsim_{\vec{n},\vec{p},\vec{r},m}1.

This shows that one has only to choose large enough
A
depending only on
\vec{n},\vec{p},\vec{r}
.

Observe that for each
R\in\mathcal{S}
and each
x\in R
we have either
N_{Q,\mathcal{F}}(x)=N_{R,\mathcal{F}}(x)
or
N_{Q,\mathcal{F}}(x)\leq A\prod_{j=1}^{m}\|W_{j}(x)A_{W_{j}^{-1},Q,t_{j}}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}\to\mathcal{H}_{j}}
. Thus, since the cubes in
\mathcal{S}
are pairwise disjoint we conclude

\displaystyle\int_{\bigcup\mathcal{S}}N_{Q,\mathcal{F}}(x)^{p}\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\sum_{R\in\mathcal{S}}\int_{R}N_{Q,\mathcal{F}}(x)^{p}\mathrm{d}x\leq cA^{p}[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p},(\vec{r},\infty),\mathrm{op}}^{p}|Q|+B\sum_{R\in\mathcal{S}}|R|

\displaystyle<\Big(cA^{p}[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p},(\vec{r},\infty),\mathrm{op}}^{p}+\frac{B}{2}\Big)|Q|.

Thus, we deduce that
B\leq 2cA^{p}[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p},(\vec{r},\infty),\mathrm{op}}^{p}+\dfrac{B}{2}
, showing that
B\leq 4cA^{p}[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p},(\vec{r},\infty),\mathrm{op}}^{p}
.
∎

We can now prove Theorem
5.4
.

Proof of Theorem
5.4
.

If
p=\infty
, note that for every cube
Q
and a.e.
x\in Q
we have

\displaystyle\prod_{j=1}^{m}\langle\|W_{j}(x)F_{j}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}^{r_{j}}\rangle_{Q}^{\frac{1}{r_{j}}}

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{j=1}^{m}\langle\|W_{j}(x)W_{j}^{-1}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}^{r_{j}}\rangle_{Q}^{\frac{1}{r_{j}}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\|F_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}_{W_{j}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H}_{j})}

\displaystyle\leq[\vec{W}]_{\vec{\infty},(\vec{r},\infty),\mathrm{op}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\|F_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}_{W_{j}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H}_{j})},

proving the result.

Now suppose
p<\infty
. Through the
3^{d}
-lattice trick and monotone convergence it suffices to prove such a bound uniformly for the operators
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{F},\vec{W},\vec{r}}
obtained by restricting the supremum in the definition of
\mathcal{M}_{\vec{W},\vec{r}}
to cubes in finite collections
\mathcal{F}
contained in a dyadic grid
\mathcal{D}
. In particular,
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{F},\vec{W},\vec{r}}(\vec{F})(x)<\infty
for every
x\in\mathbf{R}^{d}
.

For each
j\in\mathbf{Z}
, let
\mathcal{S}_{j}
be the family of all maximal cubes
R\in\mathcal{F}
with the property

2^{j}<\prod_{j=1}^{m}\langle\|(A_{W_{j}^{-1},R,t_{j}})^{-1}F_{j}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}^{r_{j}}\rangle_{R}^{\frac{1}{r_{j}}}\leq 2^{j+1},

and set

E_{j}:=\bigcup_{R\in\mathcal{S}_{j}}\{x\in R:\penalty\ M_{\mathcal{F},\vec{W},\vec{r}}(\vec{F})(x)\leq 2^{j+2}N_{R,\mathcal{F}}(x)\}.

Let
x\in\mathbf{R}^{d}
with
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{F},\vec{W},\vec{r}}(\vec{F})(x)\neq 0
. We claim that there exists
j\in\mathbf{Z}
with
x\in E_{j}
. Indeed, pick
R_{x}\in\mathcal{F}
with
x\in R_{x}
and

\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{F},\vec{W},\vec{r}}(\vec{F})(x)\leq 2\prod_{j=1}^{m}\langle\|W_{j}(x)F_{j}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}^{r_{j}}\rangle_{R_{x}}^{\frac{1}{r_{j}}}.

Observe that

\displaystyle\prod_{j=1}^{m}\langle\|W_{j}(x)F_{j}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}^{r_{j}}\rangle_{R_{x}}^{\frac{1}{r_{j}}}

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{j=1}^{m}\|W_{j}(x)A_{W_{j}^{-1},R_{x},t_{j}}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}\to\mathcal{H}_{j}}\cdot\langle\|(A_{W_{j}^{-1},R_{x},t_{j}})^{-1}F_{j}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}^{r_{j}}\rangle_{R_{x}}^{\frac{1}{r_{j}}},

thus
\prod_{j=1}^{m}\langle\|(A_{W_{j}^{-1},R_{x},t_{j}})^{-1}F_{j}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}^{r_{j}}\rangle_{R_{x}}^{\frac{1}{r_{j}}}>0
. Therefore, there exists
j\in\mathbf{Z}
with

2^{j}<\prod_{j=1}^{m}\langle\|(A_{W_{j}^{-1},R_{x},t_{j}})^{-1}F_{j}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}^{r_{j}}\rangle_{R_{x}}^{\frac{1}{r_{j}}}\leq 2^{j+1}.

Then, there is
R\in\mathcal{S}_{j}
such that
R_{x}\subseteq R
. Then
x\in R
and

\displaystyle\prod_{j=1}^{m}\langle\|W_{j}(x)F_{j}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}^{r_{j}}\rangle_{R_{x}}^{\frac{1}{r_{j}}}

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{j=1}^{m}\|W_{j}(x)A_{W_{j}^{-1},R_{x},t_{j}}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}\to\mathcal{H}_{j}}\cdot\langle\|(A_{W_{j}^{-1},R_{x},t_{j}})^{-1}F_{j}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}^{r_{j}}\rangle_{R_{x}}^{\frac{1}{r_{j}}}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{j+1}N_{R,\mathcal{F}}(x),

thus
x\in E_{j}
.

Observe also that
\bigcup\mathcal{S}_{j}\subseteq\{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathcal{F},\vec{W},\vec{r}}(\vec{F})>2^{j}\}
for each
j\in\mathbf{Z}
. Thus, we deduce

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbf{R}^{d}}M_{\mathcal{F},\vec{W},\vec{r}}(\vec{F})(x)^{p}\mathrm{d}x\leq\sum_{j\in\mathbf{Z}}\int_{E_{j}}M_{\mathcal{F},\vec{W},\vec{r}}(\vec{F})(x)^{p}\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\in\mathbf{Z}}2^{(j+2)p}\sum_{R\in\mathcal{S}_{j}}\int_{R}N_{R,\mathcal{F}}(x)^{p}\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\lesssim_{m,\vec{n},\vec{p},\vec{r}}[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p},(\vec{r},\infty),\mathrm{op}}^{p}\sum_{j\in\mathbf{Z}}2^{jp}\sum_{R\in\mathcal{S}_{j}}|R|=[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p},(\vec{r},\infty),\mathrm{op}}^{p}\sum_{j\in\mathbf{Z}}2^{jp}\Big|\bigcup\mathcal{S}_{j}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p},(\vec{r},\infty),\mathrm{op}}^{p}\sum_{j\in\mathbf{Z}}2^{jp}|\{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathcal{F},\vec{W}}(\vec{F})>2^{j}\}|\eqsim_{p}[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p},(\vec{r},\infty),\mathrm{op}}^{p}\|\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathcal{F},\vec{W},\vec{r}}(\vec{F})\|_{L^{p}(\mathbf{R}^{d})}^{p}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{d,\vec{n},\vec{p},\vec{r}}[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p},(\vec{r},\infty),\mathrm{op}}\Big(\prod_{j=1}^{m}[W_{j}^{-1}]_{\mathrm{FW}_{\frac{1}{\frac{1}{r_{j}}-\frac{1}{p_{j}}}}}^{\frac{\frac{1}{p_{j}}}{\frac{1}{r_{j}}-\frac{1}{p_{j}}}}\Big)^{p}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\|F_{j}\|_{L^{p_{j}}_{W_{j}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H}_{j}))}^{p},

concluding the proof.
∎

5.3.
Sparse domination

While we did not use this in the proof of Theorem
5.3
, we nonetheless prove convex body sparse domination of
M^{\mathcal{K}}
, generalizing
[
Nie24a
, Theorem C]
.

Lemma 5.7
.

Let
\mathcal{D}
be a dyadic grid in
\mathbf{R}^{d}
and let
\mathcal{F}\subseteq\mathcal{D}
be finite. For all
\vec{F}\in L^{\vec{1}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\vec{\mathcal{H}})
there exists a martingale
\frac{1}{2}
-sparse collection
\mathcal{S}\subseteq\mathcal{F}
for which

M^{\mathcal{K}}_{\mathcal{F}}\vec{F}(x)\subseteq n^{\frac{3}{2}}(2n)^{m}M^{\mathcal{K}}_{\mathcal{S}}\vec{F}(x)

for a.e.
x\in\mathbf{R}^{d}
.

Proof.

Let
Q_{0}\in\mathcal{F}
. Using the John ellipsoid theorem, for each
j\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
we can find an orthonormal set
\mathcal{B}_{j}(Q_{0})
of
\mathcal{H}_{j}
and
(\lambda_{e_{j}})_{e_{j}\in\mathcal{B}_{j}(Q_{0})}
,
\lambda_{e_{j}}\geq 0
, for which

\mathcal{E}_{j}(Q_{0}):=\Big\{\sum_{e_{j}\in\mathcal{B}_{j}(Q_{0})}\langle u,e_{j}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}\lambda_{e_{j}}e_{j}:\|u\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}\leq 1\Big\}

satisfies

\mathcal{E}_{j}(Q_{0})\subseteq\langle F_{j}\rangle_{Q_{0}}\subseteq n_{j}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{E}_{j}(Q_{0}).

We set

\mathcal{B}(Q_{0})=\Big\{\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}e_{j}:e_{j}\in\mathcal{B}_{j}(Q_{0}),\,j=1,\ldots,m\Big\},

and for each
e\in\mathcal{B}(Q_{0})
we let
\text{ch}_{e}(Q_{0})
denote the collection of maximal cubes
Q\in\mathcal{F}
contained in
Q_{0}
satisfying

\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\langle\langle F_{j},e_{j}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}\rangle_{Q}|>(2n)^{m}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\langle|\langle F_{j},e_{j}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}|\rangle_{Q_{0}},

where
e=\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}e_{j}
.

Letting
\text{ch}(Q_{0})
denote the maximal cubes in
\bigcup_{e\in\mathcal{B}(Q_{0})}\text{ch}_{e}(Q_{0})
, we let
\mathcal{S}_{0}
denote the maximal cubes in
\mathcal{F}
, and iteratively define

\mathcal{S}_{i+1}=\bigcup_{Q\in\mathcal{S}_{i}}\text{ch}(Q),\quad\mathcal{S}:=\bigcup_{i=0}^{\infty}\mathcal{S}_{i}.

We will show that
\mathcal{S}
is sparse. Indeed, for each
Q_{0}\in\mathcal{S}
we have

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in\text{ch}(Q_{0})}|Q|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{e\in\mathcal{B}(Q_{0})}\sum_{Q\in\text{ch}_{e}(Q_{0})}|Q|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{e\in\mathcal{B}(Q_{0})}\frac{1}{2n\prod_{j=1}^{m}\langle|\langle F_{j},e_{j}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}|\rangle_{Q_{0}}^{\frac{1}{m}}}\sum_{Q\in\text{ch}_{e}(Q_{0})}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\Big(\int_{Q}|\langle F_{j}(x),e_{j}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}|\,\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{\frac{1}{m}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{e\in\mathcal{B}(Q_{0})}\frac{1}{2n\prod_{j=1}^{m}\langle|\langle F_{j},e_{j}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}|\rangle_{Q_{0}}^{\frac{1}{m}}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\Big(\sum_{Q\in\text{ch}_{e}(Q_{0})}\int_{Q}|\langle F_{j}(x),e_{j}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}|\,\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{\frac{1}{m}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{e\in\mathcal{B}(Q_{0})}\frac{|Q_{0}|}{2n}=\frac{|Q_{0}|}{2},

as desired.

For each
Q\in\mathcal{F}
, we let
\pi_{\mathcal{S}}(Q)
denote the smallest cube
Q_{0}\in\mathcal{S}
containing
Q
. Suppose
Q\in\mathcal{F}
satisfies
\pi_{\mathcal{S}}(Q)=Q_{0}
. If
Q\neq Q_{0}
, then
Q
fails the stopping condition for all
e\in\mathcal{B}(Q_{0})
, i.e., if
e=\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}e_{j}
, then

\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\langle\langle F_{j},e_{j}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}\rangle_{Q}|\leq(2n)^{m}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\langle|\langle F_{j},e_{j}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}|\rangle_{Q_{0}}.

As this estimate remains true when
Q=Q_{0}
, we find that

(5.2)

\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}\langle F_{j}\rangle_{Q}=\sum_{e\in\mathcal{B}(Q_{0})}\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}\langle\langle F_{j},e_{j}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}\rangle_{Q}e_{j}\subseteq(2n)^{m}\sum_{e\in\mathcal{B}(Q_{0})}\Big(\prod_{j=1}^{m}\langle|\langle F_{j},e_{j}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}|\rangle_{Q_{0}}\Big)\mathcal{K}(e).

Now, fix
e\in\mathcal{B}(Q_{0})
where
e=\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}e_{j}
, and pick
f_{j}\in S^{0}(\mathbf{R}^{d};F_{j})
for which

\langle|\langle F_{j},e_{j}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}|\rangle_{Q_{0}}=\langle\langle f_{j},e_{j}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}\rangle_{Q_{0}},\quad j=1,\ldots,m.

Fixing
j\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
, since
\langle f_{j}\rangle_{Q_{0}}\in\langle F_{j}\rangle_{Q_{0}}\subseteq n_{j}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{E}_{j}(Q_{0})
, we can write

\langle f_{j}\rangle_{Q_{0}}=n_{j}^{\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{e^{\prime}_{j}\in\mathcal{B}_{j}(Q_{0})}\langle u,e^{\prime}_{j}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}\lambda_{e^{\prime}_{j}}e^{\prime}_{j}

with
\|u\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}\leq 1
. Thus,

\langle|\langle F_{j},e_{j}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}|\rangle_{Q_{0}}e_{j}=\langle\langle\langle f_{j}\rangle_{Q},e_{j}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}\rangle_{Q_{0}}e_{j}=n_{j}^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle u,e_{j}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}\lambda_{e_{j}}e_{j}\in n_{j}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{E}_{j}(Q_{0})\subseteq n_{j}^{\frac{1}{2}}\langle F_{j}\rangle_{Q_{0}}.

Combining this with (
5.2
) yields

\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}\langle F_{j}\rangle_{Q}\subseteq n^{\frac{3}{2}}(2n)^{m}\mathcal{K}\Big(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}\langle F_{j}\rangle_{Q_{0}}\Big).

In conclusion, we find that

\displaystyle M_{\mathcal{F}}\vec{F}(x)

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{Q_{0}\in\mathcal{S}}\bigcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q\in\mathcal{F}\\
\pi_{\mathcal{S}}(Q)=Q_{0}\end{subarray}}\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}\langle F_{j}\rangle_{Q}\ind_{Q}(x)\subseteq n^{\frac{3}{2}}(2n)^{m}\bigcup_{Q_{0}\in\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{K}\Big(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}\langle F_{j}\rangle_{Q_{0}}\Big)

\displaystyle\subseteq n^{\frac{3}{2}}(2n)^{m}M^{\mathcal{K}}_{\mathcal{S}}\vec{F}(x).

As
M^{\mathcal{K}}_{\mathcal{S}}\vec{F}(x)\in\mathcal{K}
, we conclude that also

M^{\mathcal{K}}_{\mathcal{F}}\vec{F}(x)=\mathcal{K}(M_{\mathcal{F}}\vec{F}(x))\subseteq n^{\frac{3}{2}}(2n)^{m}M^{\mathcal{K}}_{\mathcal{S}}\vec{F}(x),

as desired.
∎

6.
Matrix weighted bounds for multilinear Calderón–Zygmund operators

In this section we prove matrix weighted upper bounds for multilinear Calderón–Zygmund operators as well as matrix weighted lower bounds for non-degenerate multilinear Calderón–Zygmund operators.

6.1.
Multilinear Calderón–Zygmund operators

Here we briefly recall the definition of multilinear Calderón–Zygmund operators and properties of them that will be important for the convex body sparse domination.

Let
\omega:[0,\infty)\to[0,\infty)
be a
modulus of continuity
, meaning in this paper an increasing, continuous function with
\omega(0)=0
such that there is a constant
D\in[1,\infty)
with

\omega(2t)\leq D\,\omega(t),\quad\forall t\in[0,\infty).

The best such
D
is called
doubling constant of
\omega
. In particular, setting
r:=\log_{2}(D)\in[0,\infty)
, we have

\omega(ct)\leq c^{r}D\,\omega(t),\quad\forall c\in[1,\infty),\quad\forall t\in[0,\infty).

We will also assume that
\omega
satisfies the so-called
Dini condition
, that is

\|\omega\|_{\mathrm{Dini}}:=\int^{1}_{0}\!\omega(t)\,\frac{\mathrm{d}t}{t}<\infty.

Let
\Delta:=\{(y_{0},y_{1},\ldots,y_{m})\in(\mathbf{R}^{d})^{m+1}:\penalty\ y_{0}=y_{1}=\ldots=y_{m}\}
. A
m
-linear Calderón–Zygmund kernel with modulus of continuity
\omega
is a function
K:(\mathbf{R}^{d})^{m+1}\setminus\Delta\to\mathbf{C}
for which there exists a constant
C_{K}\in(0,\infty)
such that the following two conditions are fullfilled:

(1)

Size condition:
For all
(y_{0},y_{1},\ldots,y_{m})\in(\mathbf{R}^{d})^{m+1}\setminus\Delta
we have

|K(y_{0},y_{1},\ldots,y_{m})|\leq\frac{C_{K}}{\Big(\sum_{k,\ell=0}^{m}|y_{k}-y_{\ell}|\Big)^{md}}.

(2)

Smoothness condition:
For all
j=0,1,\ldots,m
, for all

(y_{0},\ldots,y_{j},\ldots,y_{m}),(y_{0},\ldots,y_{j}^{\prime},\ldots,y_{m})\in(\mathbf{R}^{d})^{m+1}\setminus\Delta

with

|y_{j}-y_{j}^{\prime}|\leq\frac{1}{2}\max_{k=0,\ldots,m}|y_{j}-y_{k}|

we have

\displaystyle|K(y_{0},\ldots,y_{j},\ldots,y_{m})-K(y_{0},\ldots,y_{j}^{\prime},\ldots,y_{m})|

\displaystyle\leq C_{K}\omega\Big(\frac{|y_{j}-y_{j}^{\prime}|}{\sum_{k,\ell=0}^{m}|y_{k}-y_{\ell}|}\Big)\cdot\frac{1}{\Big(\sum_{k,\ell=0}^{m}|y_{k}-y_{\ell}|\Big)^{md}}.

A
multilinear Calderón–Zygmund operator with kernel
K
is an
m
-linear operator

T:\mathcal{S}(\mathbf{R}^{d})^{m}\to\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbf{R}^{d}),

where
\mathcal{S}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
denotes the space of Schwarz functions on
\mathbf{R}^{d}
and
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
the space of tempered distributions, such that the following hold:

(1)

The operator
T
admits a bounded,
m
-linear extension
L^{\vec{q}}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\to L^{q}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
for some
m
-tuple
q\in[1,\infty)^{m}
. By abuse of notation we denote this extention also by
T
.

(2)

For all
m
-tuples of (suitable) complex valued functions
\vec{f}=(f_{1},\ldots,f_{m})
there holds

T(\vec{f})(x)=\int_{(\mathbf{R}^{d})^{m}}K(x,\vec{y})\prod_{j=1}^{m}f_{j}(y_{j})\,\mathrm{d}\vec{y},

for all
x\in\mathbf{R}^{d}\setminus\bigcap_{j=1}^{m}\mathrm{supp}(f_{j})
.

We will need some properties of multilinear Calderón–Zygmund operators that have appeared earlier in the literature. We recall that for
p\in(0,\infty)
and a measurable function
f:\mathbf{R}^{d}\to\mathbf{C}
we define

\|f\|_{L^{p,\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{d})}:=\sup_{\lambda>0}\lambda|\{|f|>\lambda\}|^{\frac{1}{p}}.

Proposition 6.1
.

The operator
T
admits a bounded extension
L^{\vec{1}}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\to L^{\frac{1}{m},\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
with

\|T\|_{L^{\vec{1}}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\to L^{\frac{1}{m},\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{d})}\leq C(\|T\|_{L^{\vec{q}}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\to L^{q}(\mathbf{R}^{d})}+C_{K}\|\omega\|_{\mathrm{Dini}}),

where the constant
C>0
depends only on
d,m,\vec{q}
and the doubling constant of
\omega
.

Following
[
Li18
]
, we consider the “grand maximal operator”
\mathcal{M}_{T}
given by

\mathcal{M}_{T}(\vec{f})(x):=\sup_{Q}\esssup_{\xi\in Q}|T(\vec{f})(\xi)-T(\vec{f}\ind_{3Q})(\xi)|\ind_{Q}(x),\quad x\in\mathbf{R}^{d},

where the first supremum ranges over all cubes
Q\subseteq\mathbf{R}^{d}
. Moreover, following
[
Li18
]
, for any cube
Q_{0}\subseteq\mathbf{R}^{d}
we consider the localized grand maximal operator
\mathcal{M}_{T,Q_{0}}
given by

\mathcal{M}_{T,Q_{0}}(\vec{f})(x):=\sup_{Q\subseteq Q_{0}}\esssup_{\xi\in Q}|T(\vec{f}\ind_{3Q_{0}})(\xi)-T(\vec{f}\ind_{3Q})(\xi)|\ind_{Q}(x),\quad x\in\mathbf{R}^{d},

where the first supremum ranges over all cubes
Q\subseteq\mathbf{R}^{d}
contained in
Q_{0}
. Observe that
\mathcal{M}_{T,Q_{0}}(\vec{f})\leq\mathcal{M}_{T}(\vec{f}\ind_{3Q_{0}})
.

Proposition 6.2
.

For any cube
Q_{0}\subseteq\mathbf{R}^{d}
one has

|T(\vec{f}\ind_{3Q_{0}})(x)|\leq c\|T\|_{L^{\vec{1}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\to L^{\frac{1}{m},\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{d})}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}|f_{j}(x)|+\mathcal{M}_{T,Q_{0}}(\vec{f})(x)

for a.e.
x\in Q_{0}
, where the constant
c>0
depends only on
d
.

For any
0<\eta<\infty
, we denote by
M_{\eta}
the Hardy–Littlewood
\eta
-maximal function on
\mathbf{R}^{d}
, that is, for
f\in L^{\eta}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
we define

M_{\eta}f(x):=\sup_{Q}\langle|f|^{\eta}\rangle_{Q}^{\frac{1}{\eta}}\ind_{Q}(x),\quad x\in\mathbf{R}^{d},

where the supremum ranges over all cubes
Q\subseteq\mathbf{R}^{d}
. Moreover, we denote by
\mathcal{M}
the
m
-(sub)linear maximal function on
\mathbf{R}^{d}
, that is, for
\vec{f}\in L^{\vec{1}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
we define

\mathcal{M}(\vec{f})(x):=\sup_{Q}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\langle|f_{j}|\rangle_{Q}\ind_{Q}(x),\quad x\in\mathbf{R}^{d},

where the supremum ranges over all cubes
Q\subseteq\mathbf{R}^{d}
.

Proposition 6.3
.

For all
\eta\in\Big(0,\frac{1}{m}\Big)
we have

\mathcal{M}_{T}(\vec{f})\leq C(C_{K}\|\omega\|_{\mathrm{Dini}}+\|T\|_{L^{\vec{1}}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\to L^{\frac{1}{m},\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{d})})\mathcal{M}(\vec{f})+M_{\eta}(|T(\vec{f})|)\quad\text{a.e.\penalty\ on }\mathbf{R}^{d},

where the constant
C>0
depends only on
m,d,\eta
and the doubling constant of
\omega
.

Propositions
6.1
,
6.2
and
6.3
and their proofs appear already implicitly in
[
DHL18
,
Li18
]
. Nevertheless, since the proofs there concern only the case
m=2
and especially in
[
Li18
]
a stronger,
L^{r}
-Hörmander smoothness assumption on the kernel is considered, we give detailed proofs of Propositions
6.1
,
6.2
and
6.3
in the appendix.

Corollary 6.4
.

The grand maximal operator
\mathcal{M}_{T}
maps
L^{\vec{1}}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\to L^{\frac{1}{m},\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
boundedly with

\|\mathcal{M}_{T}\|_{L^{\vec{1}}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\to L^{\frac{1}{m},\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{d})}\leq C(C_{K}\|\omega\|_{\mathrm{Dini}}+\|T\|_{L^{\vec{1}}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\to L^{\frac{1}{m},\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{d})}),

where the constant
C
depends only on
m,d
and the doubling constant of
\omega
.

Proof.

This follows immediately by combining Propositions
6.1
and
6.3
, after observing that the
m
-linear maximal operator
\mathcal{M}
maps
L^{\vec{1}}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\to L^{\frac{1}{m},\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
boundedly and that the Hardy Littlewood
\eta
-maximal function maps
L^{\frac{1}{m},\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\to L^{\frac{1}{m},\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
boundedly for any
0<\eta<\frac{1}{m}
.
∎

Remark 6.5
.

Let
\widetilde{T}
be the
m
-linear extension of
T
acting on
m
-tuples of vector valued functions as defined in (
1.3
). Then, for all
m
-tuples of (suitable) vector valued functions
\vec{f}=(f_{1},\ldots,f_{m})
with
f_{j}:\mathbf{R}^{d}\to\mathcal{H}_{j}
, we have

\widetilde{T}(\vec{f})(x)=\int_{(\mathbf{R}^{d})^{m}}\!K(x,\vec{y})\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}f_{j}(y_{j})\,\mathrm{d}\vec{y}

for all
x\in\mathbf{R}^{d}\setminus\bigcap_{j=1}^{m}\mathrm{supp}(f_{j})
.

6.2.
Sparse domination of multilinear Calderón–Zygmund operators

In this subsection we prove a pointwise domination of the vector valued extensions of multilinear Calderón–Zygmund operators by a “multilinear” convex body sparse operator. This domination is based on a bootstrapping principle whose starting point is the pointwise sparse domination algorithms in the scalar setting, which in turn relies on the following multilinear adaptation of
[
NPTV17
, Lemma 3.2]
.

For a measurable function
f:\mathbf{R}^{d}\to\mathcal{V}
that is integrable over the cube
Q
we denote
\llangle f\rrangle_{Q}:=\langle\mathcal{K}(f)\rangle_{Q}
.

Lemma 6.6
.

Let
T:L^{\vec{1}}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\to L^{0}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
be a multilinear operator. Let
\mathcal{D}
be a dyadic grid. Assume that for some cube
Q_{0}\in\mathcal{D}
there exists
\lambda\in[1,\infty)
such that the following holds. For each
0<\varepsilon<1
and for each
m
-tuple
\vec{f}\in L^{\vec{1}}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
of functions supported in
\lambda Q_{0}
, there exists a collection
\mathcal{G}
of disjoint cubes in
\mathcal{D}(Q_{0})
with the following properties:

(1)

\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{G}}|Q|\leq\varepsilon|Q_{0}|

(2)

For any family
\bar{\mathcal{G}}
of pairwise disjoint cubes in
\mathcal{D}(Q_{0})
covering
\mathcal{G}
(meaning that for each
P\in\mathcal{G}
there is
\bar{P}\in\bar{\mathcal{G}}
with
P\subseteq\bar{P}
, and no cube in
\bar{\mathcal{G}}
is a proper subset of a cube in
\mathcal{G}
) one has

\bigg|T(\vec{f})(x)-\sum_{Q\in\bar{\mathcal{G}}}\ind_{Q}(x)T(\vec{f}\ind_{\lambda Q_{0}})(x)\bigg|\leq C\prod_{j=1}^{m}\langle|f_{j}|\rangle_{\lambda Q_{0}}\quad\text{for a.e. }x\in Q_{0},

where the constant
C>0
depends only on
\lambda
,
d
,
m
,
\varepsilon
and
T
.

Then, for every
0<\delta<1
and for every
m
-tuple
\vec{f}\in L^{\vec{1}}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\vec{\mathcal{H}})
of functions supported in
\lambda Q_{0}
, there exists a collection
\mathcal{G}
of disjoint cubes in
\mathcal{D}(Q_{0})
with the following properties:

(1)

\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{G}}|Q|\leq\delta|Q_{0}|

(2)

We have

T(\vec{f})(x)\in C\mathcal{K}\Big(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}\llangle f_{j}\rrangle_{\lambda Q_{0}}\Big)+\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{G}}\ind_{Q}(x)T(\vec{f}\ind_{\lambda Q})(x)\quad\text{for a.e. }x\in Q_{0},

where the constant
C>0
depends only on
\lambda
,
d
,
m
,
\delta
,
\vec{n}
and
T
.

Proof.

Fix
0<\delta<1
and a
m
-tuple
\vec{f}\in L^{\vec{1}}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\vec{\mathcal{H}})
of functions supported in
\lambda Q_{0}
. For each
j=1,\ldots,m
, let
\mathcal{E}_{j}
be the John ellipsoid of
\llangle f_{j}\rrangle_{\lambda Q_{0}}
. Then, for each
j=1,\ldots,m
, we can find an orthonormal basis
e_{j,1},\ldots,e_{j,n_{j}}
for
\mathcal{H}_{j}
as well as
\alpha_{j,1},\ldots,\alpha_{j,n_{j}}\in[0,\infty)
, such that

\mathcal{E}_{j}:=\Big\{\sum_{k=1}^{n_{j}}x_{k}\alpha_{k}e_{k}:\penalty\ x_{k}\in\mathbf{F},\penalty\ \sum_{k=1}^{d}|x_{k}|^{2}\leq 1\Big\}.

Exactly as in
[
NPTV17
, Lemma 3.2]
we have
\langle|f_{j,k}|\rangle_{\lambda Q_{0}}\leq\sqrt{n_{j}}\,\alpha_{j,k}
for each
k=1,\ldots,n_{j}
and
j=1,\ldots,m
.

For each
\vec{k}\in\prod_{j=1}^{m}\{1,\ldots,n_{j}\}
, apply the hypothesis on the
m
-tuple
f^{\vec{k}}:=(f_{1,k_{1}},\ldots,f_{m,k_{m}})
for
\varepsilon=\frac{\delta}{n}
, where we recall that
n:=\prod_{j=1}^{m}n_{j}
. Thus, we obtain a family
\mathcal{G}^{\vec{k}}
of pairwise disjoint cubes in
\mathcal{D}(Q_{0})
, such that
\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{G}}|Q|\leq\varepsilon|Q_{0}|
and for any family
\bar{\mathcal{G}}
of pairwise disjoint cubes in
\mathcal{D}(Q_{0})
covering
\mathcal{G}
one has

\bigg|T(f^{\vec{k}})(x)-\sum_{Q\in\bar{\mathcal{G}}}\ind_{Q}(x)T(f^{\vec{k}}\ind_{\lambda Q_{0}})(x)\bigg|\leq C\prod_{j=1}^{m}\langle|f_{j,k_{j}}|\rangle_{\lambda Q_{0}}\quad\text{for a.e. }x\in Q_{0},

where the constant
C>0
depends only on
\lambda
,
d
,
m
,
\varepsilon
and
T
.

Let now
\mathcal{G}
be the family of all maximal cubes in
\bigcup_{\vec{k}}\mathcal{G}^{\vec{k}}
. Observe that

\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{G}}|Q|\leq\sum_{\vec{k}}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{G}^{\vec{k}}}|Q|\leq\sum_{\vec{k}}\varepsilon|Q_{0}|=\delta|Q_{0}|.

Moreover, observe that for each
\vec{k}
we have

\bigg|T(f^{\vec{k}})(x)-\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{G}}\ind_{Q}(x)T(f^{\vec{k}}\ind_{\lambda Q_{0}})(x)\bigg|\leq C\prod_{j=1}^{m}\langle|f_{j,k_{j}}|\rangle_{\lambda Q_{0}}\leq Cn^{\frac{1}{2}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\alpha_{j,k_{j}}

for a.e.
x\in Q_{0}
, because
\mathcal{G}
covers
\mathcal{G}^{\vec{k}}
. From the definition of the vector valued extension of
T
we deduce

T(\vec{f})(x)\in Cn^{\frac{1}{2}}P+\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{G}}\ind_{Q}(x)T(\vec{f}\ind_{\lambda Q})(x)

for a.e.
x\in Q_{0}
, where

P:=\bigg\{\sum_{\vec{k}}x_{\vec{k}}\bigg(\prod_{j=1}^{m}\alpha_{j,k_{j}}\bigg)e_{\vec{k}}:\penalty\ x_{\vec{k}}\in\mathbf{F}\text{ with }|x_{\vec{k}}|\leq 1\bigg\},

where
e_{\vec{k}}:=\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}e_{j,k_{j}}
. Observe that

P\subseteq\sqrt{n}\mathcal{K}\Big(\Big\{\lambda\bigg(\prod_{j=1}^{m}\alpha_{j,k_{j}}\bigg)e_{\vec{k}}:\penalty\ \vec{k}\in\prod_{j=1}^{m}\{1,\ldots,n_{j}\},\penalty\ \lambda\in\mathbf{F}\text{ with }|\lambda|=1\Big\}\Big).

Moreover, as

\lambda\bigg(\prod_{j=1}^{m}\alpha_{j,k_{j}}\bigg)e_{\vec{k}}\in\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}\mathcal{E}_{j}

for every
\vec{k}\in\prod_{j=1}^{m}\{1,\ldots,n_{j}\}
and
\lambda\in\mathbf{F}
with
|\lambda|=1
, it follows that

P\subseteq n^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{K}\Big(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}\mathcal{E}_{j}\Big)\subseteq n^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{K}\Big(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}\llangle f_{j}\rrangle_{\lambda Q_{0}}\Big),

concluding the proof.
∎

Lemma 6.7
.

Let
T
be a
m
-linear Calderón–Zygmund operator with a kernel having a modulus of continuity that satisfies the Dini condition. Let
\mathcal{D}
be a dyadic grid in
\mathbf{R}^{d}
. Then, for any cube
Q_{0}\in\mathcal{D}
, the assumptions of Lemma
6.6
are satisfied with
\lambda=3
.

Proof.

We adapt
[
NPTV17
, 3.2.2]
. Let
Q\in\mathcal{D}
and
0<\varepsilon<1
be arbitrary. Let
\vec{f}\in L^{\vec{1}}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
be a
m
-tuple of functions supported in
3Q_{0}
. Then, by Corollary
6.4
and Chebyshev’s inequality, we have that there exists a constant
C_{1}>0
depending only on
T
,
d
and
m
, such that for the set

\displaystyle E

\displaystyle:=\Big\{x\in Q_{0}:\penalty\ \mathcal{M}_{T,Q_{0}}(\vec{f})(x)>\frac{C_{1}}{\varepsilon^{m}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\langle|f_{j}|\rangle_{3Q_{0}}\Big\}

\displaystyle\cup\Big(\bigcup_{j=1}^{m}\Big\{x\in Q_{0}:\penalty\ |f_{j}(x)|>\frac{C_{1}}{\varepsilon}\langle|f_{j}|\rangle_{3Q_{0}}\Big\}\Big)

we have
|E|<\frac{\varepsilon}{2^{d+1}}|Q_{0}|
. Then, for all
x\in Q_{0}\setminus E
, by Proposition
6.2
we have

T(\vec{f})(x)\leq\frac{C_{2}}{\varepsilon^{m}}\prod_{j=1}^{n}\langle|f_{j}|\rangle_{3Q_{0}},

where the constant
C_{2}>0
depends only on
T
,
d
and
m
.

Let now
\mathcal{G}
be the family of all maximal cubes
Q\in\mathcal{D}(Q_{0})
such that

\langle\ind_{E}\rangle_{Q}>\frac{1}{2^{d+1}}.

Let
\bar{\mathcal{G}}
be an arbitrary family of pairwise disjoint cubes in
\mathcal{D}(Q_{0})
covering
\mathcal{G}
. Since
\Big|E\setminus\Big(\bigcup_{Q\in\mathcal{G}}Q\Big)\Big|=0
by the Lebesgue differentiation theorem, we conclude that

T(\vec{f})(x)\leq\frac{C_{2}}{\varepsilon^{m}}\prod_{j=1}^{n}\langle|f_{j}|\rangle_{3Q_{0}},

for a.e.
x\in Q_{0}\setminus\Big(\bigcup_{Q\in\mathcal{G}}Q\Big)
. In particular, we have

T(\vec{f})(x)\leq\frac{C_{2}}{\varepsilon^{m}}\prod_{j=1}^{n}\langle|f_{j}|\rangle_{3Q_{0}},

for a.e.
x\in Q_{0}\setminus\Big(\bigcup_{Q\in\bar{\mathcal{G}}}Q\Big)
.

Let now
Q\in\bar{\mathcal{G}}
be arbitrary. We show that

|T(\vec{f})(x)-T(\vec{f}\mathbf{1}_{3Q_{0}})(x)|\leq\frac{C_{1}}{\varepsilon^{m}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\langle|f_{j}|\rangle_{3Q_{0}}

for a.e.
x\in Q
.

If
Q=Q_{0}
, then
\langle\ind_{E}\rangle_{Q}\leq\frac{1}{2}
. If
Q\neq Q_{0}
, then by the maximality of the cubes in
\mathcal{G}
we deduce that the parent
\hat{Q}
of
Q
in
\mathcal{D}
, which is contained in
Q_{0}
, satisfies
\langle\ind_{E}\rangle_{\hat{Q}}\leq\frac{1}{2^{d+1}}
, therefore
\langle\ind_{E}\rangle_{Q}\leq\frac{1}{2}
. In particular,
Q
cannot be a subset of
E
, and therefore there exists
x_{0}\in Q
with
\mathcal{M}_{T,Q_{0}}(\vec{f})(x_{0})\leq\frac{C_{1}}{\varepsilon^{m}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\langle|f_{j}|\rangle_{3Q_{0}}
. Since
x_{0}\in Q
and
Q\subseteq Q_{0}
, from the definition of the localized grand maximal operator we deduce

|T(\vec{f}\ind_{3Q_{0}})(x)-T(\vec{f}\ind_{3Q})(x)|\leq\frac{C_{1}}{\varepsilon^{m}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\langle|f_{j}|\rangle_{3Q_{0}}\quad\text{for a.e. }x\in Q,

in other words

|T(\vec{f})(x)-T(\vec{f}\ind_{3Q})(x)|\leq\frac{C_{1}}{\varepsilon^{m}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\langle|f_{j}|\rangle_{3Q_{0}}\quad\text{for a.e. }x\in Q,

concluding the proof.
∎

Lemma 6.8
.

Let
\mathcal{S}
be an
\eta
-sparse family of cubes in
\mathbf{R}^{d}
for some
0<\eta<1
and let
\vec{F}\in L^{\vec{1}}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{K}(\vec{\mathcal{H}}))
. Then, for a.e.
x\in\mathbf{R}^{d}
, the Minkowski sum

\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{K}\Big(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}\langle F_{j}\rangle_{Q}\Big)\mathbf{1}_{Q}(x)

is well-defined as a bounded, convex, complex symmetric subset of
\mathcal{H}
.

Proof.

This is an adaptation of
[
NPTV17
, Lemma 2.5]
. It suffices to show that

\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{S}}\Big\|\mathcal{K}\Big(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}\langle F_{j}\rangle_{Q}\Big)\Big\|_{\mathcal{H}}\mathbf{1}_{Q}(x)<\infty\quad\text{for a.e. }x\in\mathbf{R}^{d},

in other words that

\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{S}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\|\langle F_{j}\rangle_{Q}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}\ind_{Q}(x)<\infty\quad\text{for a.e. }x\in\mathbf{R}^{d}.

In view of Proposition
2.7
, it suffices to show that

\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{S}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\langle\|F_{j}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}\rangle_{Q}\ind_{Q}(x)<\infty\quad\text{for a.e. }x\in\mathbf{R}^{d}.

By a standard application of the
3^{d}
-lattice trick, we can without loss of generality assume that
\mathcal{S}
is a subset of some dyadic grid
\mathcal{D}
in
\mathbf{R}^{d}
. Set

\mathcal{S}_{1}:=\{Q\in\mathcal{S}:\penalty\ \ell(Q)\leq 1\},\quad\mathcal{S}_{2}:=\mathcal{S}\setminus\mathcal{S}_{1}.

Exactly as in
[
NPTV17
, Lemma 2.5]
we have that the set
\{Q\in\mathcal{S}_{1}:\penalty\ x\in Q\}
is finite, for a.e.
x\in\mathbf{R}^{d}
. Thus, we only have to prove that

\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{S}_{2}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\langle\|F_{j}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}\rangle_{Q}\ind_{Q}(x)<\infty\quad\text{for a.e. }x\in\mathbf{R}^{d}.

It is clear that there exists a finite subset
\mathcal{F}
of
\mathcal{D}
such that
\ell(Q)=2
, for all
Q\in\mathcal{S}
and

\bigcup_{j=1}^{m}\mathrm{supp}(F_{j})\subseteq\bigcup_{Q\in\mathcal{F}}Q.

Thus, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{S}_{2}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\langle\|F_{j}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}\rangle_{Q}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}R\in\mathcal{D}\\
Q\subseteq\mathbf{R}\end{subarray}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\langle\|F_{j}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}\rangle_{Q}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{k=2}^{\infty}2^{-mdk}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\int_{\mathbf{R}^{d}}\|F_{j}(x)\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}\mathrm{d}x<\infty,

concluding the proof.
∎

If
\mathcal{S}
is an
\eta
-sparse family of cubes in
\mathbf{R}^{d}
for some
0<\eta<1
, then for we consider the
multilinear convex body sparse operator

A_{\mathcal{S}}
acting on
m
-tuples
\vec{F}\in L^{\vec{1}}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{K}(\vec{\mathcal{H}}))
by

A_{\mathcal{S}}(\vec{F})(x):=\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{K}\Big(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}\langle F_{j}\rangle_{Q}\Big)\mathbf{1}_{Q}(x)\quad\text{for\penalty\ a.e. }x\in\mathbf{R}^{d}.

By Lemma
6.8
and
[
BC23
, Theorem 3.3]
we have that
A_{\mathcal{S}}(\vec{F}):\mathbf{R}^{d}\to\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H})
is a well-defined measurable map. For
\vec{f}\in L^{\vec{1}}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\vec{\mathcal{H}})
we denote
A_{\mathcal{S}}(\vec{f}):=A_{\mathcal{S}}(\mathcal{K}(\vec{f}))
.

Theorem 6.9
.

Let
T
be a
m
-linear Calderón–Zygmund operator with a kernel having a modulus of continuity that satisfies the Dini condition. Then, for any cube
Q_{0}\subseteq\mathbf{R}^{d}
and for any
m
-tuple of functions
\vec{f}\in L^{\vec{1}}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\vec{\mathcal{H}})
supported in
Q_{0}
, there exists a martingale
\frac{1}{2\cdot 3^{d}}
-sparse family
\mathcal{S}
of cubes in
\mathbf{R}^{d}
contained in
3Q_{0}
such that

T(\vec{f})(x)\in CA_{\mathcal{S}}(\vec{f})(x)\quad\text{for a.e. }x\in Q_{0},

where the constant
C
depends only on
d
,
m
,
\vec{n}
and
T
.

Proof.

We adapt
[
NPTV17
, Theorem 3.4]
. Let
\vec{f}\in L^{\vec{1}}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\vec{\mathcal{H}})
be a
m
-tuple of functions supported in
Q_{0}
. Let
\mathcal{D}
be a dyadic grid with
Q_{0}\in\mathcal{D}
. Set
\mathcal{G}_{0}:=\{Q_{0}\}
. We apply Lemma
6.7
for the cube
Q_{0}
and the
m
-tuple
\vec{f}
with
\delta=\frac{1}{2}
. Thus, we obtain a family
\mathcal{G}_{1}
of pairwise disjoint cubes in
\mathcal{D}(Q_{0})
such that

\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}|Q|\leq\frac{1}{2}|Q_{0}|

and

T(\vec{f})(x)\in C\mathcal{K}\Big(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}\llangle f_{j}\rrangle_{3Q_{0}}\Big)+\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\ind_{Q}(x)T(\vec{f}\ind_{3Q})(x)\quad\text{for a.e. }x\in Q_{0},

where the constant
C>0
depends only on
d
,
m
,
\vec{n}
and
T
.

Next, for each
Q_{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{1}
, we repeat this step for the cube
Q_{1}
and the
m
-tuple
\vec{f}\ind_{3Q_{1}}
with
\delta=\frac{1}{2}
, obtaining a family
\mathcal{G}_{2,Q_{1}}
of pairwise disjoint cubes in
\mathcal{D}(Q_{1})
such that

\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{G}_{2,Q_{1}}}|Q|\leq\frac{1}{2}|Q_{1}|

and

T(\vec{f})(x)\in C\mathcal{K}\Big(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}\llangle f_{j}\rrangle_{3Q_{1}}\Big)+\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{G}_{2,Q_{2}}}\ind_{Q}(x)T(\vec{f}\ind_{3Q})(x)\quad\text{for a.e. }x\in Q_{1},

with the same constant
C
as above. Setting
\mathcal{G}_{2}:=\bigcup_{Q\in\mathcal{G}_{1}}\mathcal{G}_{2,Q}
we deduce

T(\vec{f})(x)\in C\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{G}_{0}\cup\mathcal{G}_{1}}\mathcal{K}\Big(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}\llangle f_{j}\rrangle_{3Q}\Big)\ind_{Q}(x)+\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{G}_{2}}\ind_{Q}(x)T(\vec{f}\ind_{3Q})(x)\quad\text{for a.e. }x\in Q_{0}.

Now we repeat this steps for the cubes in
\mathcal{G}_{2}
and continue this process inductively, obtaining successively families
\mathcal{G}_{k}
,
k=1,2,3,\ldots
. After the
k
-th step we have

T(\vec{f})(x)\in C\sum_{Q\in\bigcup_{\ell=0}^{k-1}\mathcal{G}_{\ell}}\mathcal{K}\Big(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}\llangle f_{j}\rrangle_{3Q}\Big)\ind_{Q}(x)+\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{G}_{k}}\ind_{Q}(x)T(\vec{f}\ind_{3Q})(x)\quad\text{for a.e. }x\in Q_{0},

for all
k=1,2,\ldots
. Set
\mathcal{G}:=\bigcup_{k=0}^{\infty}\mathcal{G}_{k}
. Since

\Big|\bigcap_{k=1}^{\infty}\bigcup_{Q\in\mathcal{G}_{k}}Q\Big|=\lim_{k\to\infty}\Big|\bigcup_{Q\in\mathcal{G}_{k}}Q\Big|=0,

we deduce that

T(\vec{f})(x)\in C\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{G}}\mathcal{K}\Big(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}\llangle f_{j}\rrangle_{3Q}\Big)\ind_{Q}(x)\quad\text{for a.e. }x\in Q_{0}.

Set
\mathcal{S}:=\{3Q:\penalty\ Q\in\mathcal{G}\}
. Then,
\mathcal{S}
is a martingale
\frac{1}{2\cdot 3^{d}}
-sparse family with

T(\vec{f})(x)\in C\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{K}\Big(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}\llangle f_{j}\rrangle_{Q}\Big)\ind_{Q}(x)\quad\text{for a.e. }x\in Q_{0},

concluding the proof.
∎

6.3.
Matrix weighted bounds for multilinear convex body sparse operators

In this subsection we prove matrix weighted bounds for our “multilinear” convex body sparse operator.

For each
C\in\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{V})
and
v\in\mathcal{V}
we denote

\langle C,v\rangle_{\mathcal{V}}:=\{\langle u,v\rangle_{\mathcal{V}}:\penalty\ u\in\mathcal{K}\}\subseteq\mathbf{F}.

Then we have
\langle C,v\rangle_{\mathcal{V}}\in\mathcal{K}(\mathbf{F})
.

Theorem 6.10
.

Let
\vec{p}\in(1,\infty]^{m}
satisfy
\tfrac{1}{p}:=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\tfrac{1}{p_{j}}>0
, and let
\vec{W}\in A_{\vec{p}}
. If
\mathcal{S}
is an
\eta
-sparse collection of cubes in
\mathbf{R}^{d}
for some
0<\eta<1
, then

\displaystyle\|A_{\mathcal{S}}\|_{L^{\vec{p}}_{\vec{W}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{K}(\vec{\mathcal{H}}))\to L^{p}_{\mathbf{W}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{H}))}\lesssim_{d,m,\vec{n},\vec{p},\eta}[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p}}[\mathbf{W}]_{\mathrm{FW}_{p}}^{(p-1)^{+}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}[W_{j}^{-1}]_{\mathrm{FW}_{p_{j}^{\prime}}}^{\frac{p_{j}^{\prime}}{p_{j}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{d,m,\vec{n},p}[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p}}^{\max(1,p)+\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{p_{j}^{\prime}}{p_{j}}}.

Proof of Theorem
6.10
.

The second bound follows immediately from Theorem
4.4
(and Remark
4.14
), so we only have to prove the first.

First of all, by monotone convergence (see
[
Nie24a
, Proposition 3.7]
), we may assume that
\mathcal{S}
is finite. For each cube
Q\subseteq\mathbf{R}^{d}
we let
A_{j,Q}:=A_{W_{j}^{-1},Q,p_{j}^{\prime}}
, and
A_{Q}:=A_{\mathbf{W},Q,p}
. We now distinguish two cases.

Case 1.:
p\geq 1
. Let
h\in L^{p^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{W}^{-1}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{H})
be arbitrary. We need to show that

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{\mathbf{R}^{d}}\langle g(x),h(x)\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\mathrm{d}x\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{d,m,\vec{n},\vec{p},\varepsilon}[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p}}[\mathbf{W}]_{\mathrm{FW}_{p}}^{(p-1)^{+}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}[W_{j}^{-1}]_{\mathrm{FW}_{p_{j}^{\prime}}}^{\frac{p_{j}^{\prime}}{p_{j}}}\|\vec{F}\|_{L^{\vec{p}}_{\vec{W}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{K}(\vec{\mathcal{H}}))}\|h\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{W}^{-1}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{H})},

for every
g\in S^{0}(\mathbf{R}^{d};A_{\mathcal{S}}(\vec{F}))
. It is clear that

\Big|\int_{\mathbf{R}^{d}}\langle g(x),h(x)\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\mathrm{d}x\Big|\leq\int_{\mathbf{R}^{d}}|\langle A_{\mathcal{S}}(\vec{F})(x),h(x)\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}|\mathrm{d}x,

for any
g\in S^{0}(\mathbf{R}^{d};A_{\mathcal{S}}(\vec{F}))
. Thus, we only have to bound
\int_{\mathbf{R}^{d}}|\langle A_{\mathcal{S}}(\vec{F})(x),h(x)\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}|\mathrm{d}x
. We estimate

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbf{R}^{d}}|\langle A_{\mathcal{S}}(\vec{F})(x),h(x)\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}|\mathrm{d}x=\int_{\mathbf{R}^{d}}\Big|\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{S}}\Big\langle\mathcal{K}\Big(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}\langle F_{j}\rangle_{Q}\Big),h(x)\Big\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\ind_{Q}(x)\Big|\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbf{R}^{d}}\Big|\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{S}}\Big\langle A_{Q}\Big(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}A_{j,Q}\Big)\mathcal{K}\Big(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}\langle A_{j,Q}^{-1}F_{j}\rangle_{Q}\Big),A_{Q}^{-1}h(x)\Big\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\ind_{Q}(x)\Big|\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\lesssim_{m,\vec{n},\vec{p}}[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p}}\int_{\mathbf{R}^{d}}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{S}}\Big\|\mathcal{K}\Big(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}\langle A_{j,Q}^{-1}F_{j}\rangle_{Q}\Big)\Big\|_{\mathcal{H}}\cdot\|A_{Q}^{-1}h(x)\|_{\mathcal{H}}\ind_{Q}(x)\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p}}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{S}}\Big\|\mathcal{K}\Big(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}\langle A_{j,Q}^{-1}F_{j}\rangle_{Q}\Big)\Big\|_{\mathcal{H}}\cdot\langle\|A_{Q}^{-1}h\|_{\mathcal{H}}\rangle_{Q}\cdot|Q|

\displaystyle\leq[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p}}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{S}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\langle\|A_{j,Q}^{-1}F_{j}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}\rangle_{Q}\cdot\langle\|A_{Q}^{-1}h\|_{\mathcal{H}}\rangle_{Q}\cdot|Q|

\displaystyle\leq[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p}}\Big(\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{S}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\langle\|A_{j,Q}^{-1}F_{j}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}\rangle_{Q}^{p}|Q|\Big)^{\frac{1}{p}}\Big(\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{S}}\langle\|A_{Q}^{-1}h\|_{\mathcal{H}}\rangle_{Q}^{p^{\prime}}|Q|\Big)^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}}.

Using Proposition
5.5
we obtain

\displaystyle\Big(\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{S}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\langle\|A_{j,Q}^{-1}F_{j}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}\rangle_{Q}^{p}|Q|\Big)^{\frac{1}{p}}\lesssim_{\eta,p}\Big(\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{S}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\langle\|A_{j,Q}^{-1}F_{j}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}\rangle_{Q}^{p}|E_{Q}|\Big)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\vec{W}}(\vec{F})\|_{L^{p}(\mathbf{R}^{d})}\lesssim_{d,m,\vec{n},\vec{p}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}[W_{j}^{-1}]_{\mathrm{FW}_{p_{j}^{\prime}}}^{\frac{p_{j}^{\prime}}{p_{j}}}\|\vec{F}\|_{L^{\vec{p}}_{\vec{W}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{K}(\vec{\mathcal{H}}))}.

As for the last term, let
r\in(1,\infty)
with

r^{\prime}=2^{d+1}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}A\in\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{C}^{\vec{n}})\\
A\text{ invertible}\end{subarray}}[\|\mathbf{W}A\|_{\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{H}}^{p}]_{\mathrm{FW}},

where as a consequence of Lemma
2.6
we observe that

\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}A\in\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{C}^{\vec{n}})\\
A\text{ invertible}\end{subarray}}[\|\mathbf{W}A\|_{\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{H}}^{p}]^{\frac{1}{p}}_{\mathrm{FW}}\lesssim_{n}[\mathbf{W}]_{\mathrm{FW}_{p}}<\infty.

Thus, by the sharp reverse Hölder inequality
[
HPR12
, Theorem 2.3]
, we obtain

\displaystyle\Big(\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{S}}\langle\|A_{Q}^{-1}h\|_{\mathcal{H}}\rangle_{Q}^{p^{\prime}}|Q|\Big)^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}}\leq\Big(\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{S}}\langle\|A_{Q}^{-1}\mathbf{W}\|_{\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{H}}\cdot\|\mathbf{W}h\|_{\mathcal{H}}\rangle_{Q}^{p^{\prime}}|Q|\Big)^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{S}}\langle\|A_{Q}^{-1}\mathbf{W}\|_{\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{H}}^{rp}\rangle_{Q}^{\frac{p^{\prime}}{rp}}\cdot\langle\|\mathbf{W}h\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{(rp)^{\prime}}\rangle_{Q}^{\frac{p^{\prime}}{(rp)^{\prime}}}|Q|\Big)^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{d,p}\Big(\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{S}}\langle\|A_{Q}^{-1}\mathbf{W}\|_{\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{H}}^{p}\rangle_{Q}^{\frac{p^{\prime}}{p}}\cdot\langle\|\mathbf{W}h\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{(rp)^{\prime}}\rangle_{Q}^{\frac{p^{\prime}}{(rp)^{\prime}}}|Q|\Big)^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{n,p}\Big(\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{S}}\langle\|\mathbf{W}h\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{(rp)^{\prime}}\rangle_{Q}^{\frac{p^{\prime}}{(rp)^{\prime}}}|Q|\Big)^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}}\lesssim_{\varepsilon,p}\Big(\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{S}}\langle\|\mathbf{W}h\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{(rp)^{\prime}}\rangle_{Q}^{\frac{p^{\prime}}{(rp)^{\prime}}}|E_{Q}|\Big)^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\int_{\mathbf{R}^{d}}M(\|\mathbf{W}h\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{(rp)^{\prime}})(x)^{\frac{p^{\prime}}{(rp)^{\prime}}}\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}},

where
M
is the usual Hardy–Littlewood maximal function on
\mathbf{R}^{d}
. Note that
\frac{p^{\prime}}{(rp)^{\prime}}>1
, because
r>1
. Thus, we have

\Big(\int_{\mathbf{R}^{d}}M(\|\mathbf{W}h\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{(rp)^{\prime}})(x)^{\frac{p^{\prime}}{(rp)^{\prime}}}\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}}\lesssim_{d,p}\Big[\Big(\frac{p^{\prime}}{(rp)^{\prime}}\Big)^{\prime}\Big]^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}}\|h\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{H})}.

As in the proof of Proposition
5.5
we have

\Big[\Big(\frac{p^{\prime}}{(rp)^{\prime}}\Big)^{\prime}\Big]^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}}\lesssim_{n,d,p}[\mathbf{W}]_{\mathrm{FW}_{p}}^{\frac{p}{p^{\prime}}},

concluding the proof in this case.

Case 2.:
p<1
. Using that

\|x\|_{\ell^{1}(\mathbf{N})}\leq\|x\|_{\ell^{p}(\mathbf{N})},

for all sequences
x=(x_{k})_{k\in\mathbf{N}}
of real numbers, we estimate

\displaystyle\|A_{\mathcal{S}}(\vec{F})\|_{L^{p}_{\mathbf{W}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{H})}^{p}=\int_{\mathbf{R}^{d}}\Big\|\mathbf{W}(x)\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{K}\Big(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}\langle F_{j}\rangle_{Q}\Big)\ind_{Q}(x)\Big\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbf{R}^{d}}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{S}}\Big\|\mathbf{W}(x)\mathcal{K}\Big(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}\langle F_{j}\rangle_{Q}\Big)\Big\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{p}\ind_{Q}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbf{R}^{d}}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{S}}\Big\|\mathbf{W}(x)\Big(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}A_{j,Q}\Big)\Big\|_{\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{H}}^{p}\cdot\Big\|\mathcal{K}\Big(\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}\langle A_{j,Q}^{-1}F_{j}\rangle_{Q}\Big)\Big\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{p}\ind_{Q}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\lesssim_{p,m,\vec{n}}[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p}}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{S}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\langle\|A_{j,Q}^{-1}F_{j}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}\rangle_{Q}^{p}|Q|.

Using now the sparseness of
\mathcal{S}
and then Proposition
5.5
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{S}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\langle\|A_{j,Q}^{-1}F_{j}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}\rangle_{Q}^{p}|Q|\lesssim_{\eta}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{S}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\langle\|A_{j,Q}^{-1}F_{j}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}\rangle_{Q}^{p}|E_{Q}|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbf{R}^{d}}\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\vec{W}}(\vec{F})(x)^{p}\mathrm{d}x\lesssim_{d,m,\vec{n},\vec{p}}\Big(\prod_{j=1}^{m}[W_{j}^{-1}]_{\mathrm{FW}_{p_{j}^{\prime}}}^{\frac{p_{j}^{\prime}}{p_{j}}}\Big)^{p}\|\vec{F}\|_{L^{\vec{p}}_{\vec{W}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{K}(\vec{\mathcal{H}}))}^{p},

concluding the proof.
∎

Corollary 6.11
.

Let
T
be a
m
-linear Calderón–Zygmund operator with a kernel having a modulus of continuity that satisfies the Dini condition. Let
\vec{p}\in(1,\infty]^{m}
with
p<\infty
, and let
\vec{W}\in A_{\vec{p}}
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\|T\|_{L^{\vec{p}}_{\vec{W}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\vec{\mathcal{H}})\to L^{p}_{\mathbf{W}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{H})}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{T,d,m,\vec{n},\vec{p}}[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p}}[\mathbf{W}]_{\mathrm{FW}_{p}}^{(p-1)^{+}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}[W_{j}^{-1}]_{\mathrm{FW}_{p_{j}^{\prime}}}^{\frac{p_{j}^{\prime}}{p_{j}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{d,m,\vec{n},\vec{p}}[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p}}^{\max(1,p)+\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{p_{j}^{\prime}}{p_{j}}}.

Proof.

Let
\vec{f}\in L^{\vec{1}}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\vec{\mathcal{H}})
be arbitrary. By monotone convergence it suffices to prove that for every cube
Q_{0}\subseteq\mathbf{R}^{d}
containing
\bigcup_{j=1}^{m}\mathrm{supp}(f_{j})
we have

\Big(\int_{Q_{0}}\|\mathbf{W}(x)T(\vec{f})(x)\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{p}\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{\frac{1}{p}}\lesssim_{T,d,m,\vec{n},\vec{p}}[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p}}[\mathbf{W}]_{\mathrm{FW}_{p}}^{(p-1)^{+}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}[W_{j}^{-1}]_{\mathrm{FW}_{p_{j}^{\prime}}}^{\frac{p_{j}^{\prime}}{p_{j}}}\|\vec{f}\|_{L^{\vec{p}}_{\vec{W}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\vec{\mathcal{H})}}.

Let
Q_{0}\subseteq\mathbf{R}^{d}
be any cube containing
\bigcup_{j=1}^{m}\mathrm{supp}(f_{j})
. By Theorem
6.9
we have that there exists a martingale
\frac{1}{2\cdot 3^{d}}
-sparse family
\mathcal{S}
of cubes in
\mathbf{R}^{d}
such that

T(\vec{f})(x)\in CA_{\mathcal{S}}(\vec{f})(x)\quad\text{for a.e. }x\in Q_{0},

where the constant
C
depends only on
T,d,m
and
\vec{n}
. Thus, by Theorem
6.10
we obtain

\displaystyle\Big(\int_{Q_{0}}\|\mathbf{W}(x)T(\vec{f})(x)\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{p}\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{\frac{1}{p}}\lesssim_{T,d,m,\vec{n}}\Big(\int_{Q_{0}}\|\mathbf{W}(x)A_{\mathcal{S}}(\vec{f})(x)\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{p}\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{d,m,\vec{n},\vec{p}}[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p}}[\mathbf{W}]_{\mathrm{FW}_{p}}^{(p-1)^{+}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}[W_{j}^{-1}]_{\mathrm{FW}_{p_{j}^{\prime}}}^{\frac{p_{j}^{\prime}}{p_{j}}}\|\vec{f}\|_{L^{\vec{p}}_{\vec{W}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\vec{\mathcal{H}})},

concluding the proof.
∎

6.4.
Non-degeneracy

Finally, we prove that the boundedness of non-degenerate
m
-linear Calderón–Zygmund operators implies the multilinear Muckenhoupt condition.

Such a result for matrix weights in the case
m=1
is proven in
[
Gol03
, Theorem 5.2]
. However, to treat the case
m>1
, we require an idea that is more involved. In
[
Gol03
]
, the operator
T
is applied twice in order to obtain an operator satisfying appropriate estimates with respect to the averaging operator, whose boundedness characterizes the Muckenhoupt condition. In the multilinear setting, an operator maps
m
functions into a single function and, hence, cannot be applied twice. In order to deal with this, we will split the argument into two steps in a way similar to what is done in
[
Nie24b
,
Ler24
]
, and use a general factorization technique to reduce
m
functions back to one.

Definition 6.12
.

Let
T
be an
m
-linear Calderón–Zygmund operator. We call
T

directionally non-degenerate
if there is a
C>0
such that for all cubes
Q
there is a cube
Q^{\prime}
satisfying the following properties:

(1)

For all
0\leq h_{1},\ldots,h_{m}\in L^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
supported in
Q^{\prime}
with
\prod_{j=1}^{m}h_{j}=\ind_{Q^{\prime}}
, we have

1\leq C|T(\vec{h})(x)|

for a.e.
x\in Q
.

(2)

For all
0<\alpha<1
there is a mapping
S:Q^{\prime}\times Q^{m}\to\mathbf{C}
satisfying

|S(x,\vec{y})|\leq|Q|^{-m}

a.e. for which for all
\vec{f}\in L^{\vec{\infty}}_{c}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\vec{\mathcal{H}})
supported in
Q
we have

\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}\langle f_{j}\rangle_{Q}=(1-\alpha)C\widetilde{T}(\vec{f})(x)+\alpha\int_{Q^{m}}S(x,\vec{y})\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}f_{j}(y_{j})\,\mathrm{d}\vec{y}

for a.e.
x\in Q^{\prime}
.

Our main result of this section is as follows:

Theorem 6.13
.

Let
\vec{p}\in(1,\infty]^{m}
with
p<\infty
and let
\vec{W}
be matrix weights for which
W_{j}^{-1}
is locally
p_{j}^{\prime}
-integrable for
j=1,\ldots,m
. If
T
is a non-degenerate
m
-linear Calderón–Zygmund operator satisfying

\widetilde{T}:L^{\vec{p}}_{\vec{W}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\vec{\mathcal{H}})\to L^{p}_{\mathbf{W}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{H}),

then
\vec{W}\in A_{\vec{p}}
, with

[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p}}\lesssim\|\widetilde{T}\|_{L^{\vec{p}}_{\vec{W}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\vec{\mathcal{H}})\to L^{p}_{\mathbf{W}}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{H})}^{2}.

Before we get to the proof, we first define a scalar multilinear non-degeneracy condition which reduces back to
[
Ler24
, Definition 3.11]
when
m=1
.

Definition 6.14
.

Let
T
be an
m
-linear Calderón–Zygmund operator. We call
T

non-degenerate
if there is a
C>0
such that for all cubes
Q
there is a cube
Q^{\prime}
satisfying the following properties:

(1)

For all
0\leq h_{1},\ldots,h_{m}\in L^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
supported in
Q^{\prime}
with
\prod_{j=1}^{m}h_{j}=\ind_{Q^{\prime}}
, we have

1\leq C|T(\vec{h})(x)|

for a.e.
x\in Q
.

(2)

For all non-negative
\vec{h}\in L^{\vec{\infty}}_{c}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
supported in
Q
we have

\prod_{j=1}^{m}\langle h_{j}\rangle_{Q}\leq C|T(\vec{h})(x)|

for a.e.
x\in Q^{\prime}
.

The boundedness of such an operator implies the scalar Muckenhoupt condition. As a matter of fact, we only need a weak-type boundedness, where

\|f\|_{X_{\text{weak}}}:=\sup_{\lambda>0}\|\ind_{\{|f|>\lambda\}}\lambda\|_{X}.

Note that for any
E\subseteq\mathbf{R}^{d}
we have
\|\ind_{E}\|_{X}=\|\ind_{E}\|_{X_{\text{weak}}}
.

Proposition 6.15
.

Let
\vec{X}
be Banach function spaces over
\mathbf{R}^{d}
with the Fatou property and let
T
be an
m
-linear non-degenerate Calderón–Zygmund operator for which

T:\vec{X}\to X_{\emph{weak}}.

Then
T_{Q}:\vec{X}\to X
uniformly for all cubes
Q
, with

\sup_{Q}\|T_{Q}\|_{\vec{X}\to X}\leq C^{2}\|T\|^{2}_{\vec{X}\to X_{\emph{weak}}}.

In particular,
\ind_{Q}\in X
and
\ind_{Q}\in X_{j}^{\prime}
for
j=1,\ldots,m
for all cubes
Q
.

Proof.

Let
Q
be a cube, let
Q^{\prime}
be as in the definition of the non-degeneracy of
T
, and let
0\leq h_{1},\ldots,h_{m}\in L^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
supported in
Q^{\prime}
with
\prod_{j=1}^{m}h_{j}=\ind_{Q^{\prime}}
. Then, by property
1
and the ideal property of
X_{\text{weak}}
,
\ind_{Q^{\prime}}\in X
with

\|\ind_{Q}\|_{X}=\|\ind_{Q^{\prime}}\|_{X_{\text{weak}}}\leq C\|T\|_{\vec{X}\to X_{\text{weak}}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\|h_{j}\|_{X_{j}}.

By the ideal property of the
X_{j}
, this is true for all
0\leq h_{j}\in X_{j}
with
\ind_{Q^{\prime}}\leq\prod_{j=1}^{m}h_{j}
. Thus, taking an infimum over such
h_{j}
, we conclude from the definition of
X=\prod_{j=1}^{m}X_{j}
that

\|\ind_{Q}\|_{X}=\|\ind_{Q^{\prime}}\|_{X_{\text{weak}}}\leq C\|T\|_{\vec{X}\to X_{\text{weak}}}\|\ind_{Q^{\prime}}\|_{X}.

Now, let
\vec{f}\in L^{\vec{\infty}}_{c}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\cap\vec{X}
. Then the above estimate combined with property
2
yields

\displaystyle\|T_{Q}(\vec{f})\|_{X}

\displaystyle=\prod_{j=1}^{m}\langle f_{j}\rangle_{Q}\|\ind_{Q}\|_{X}\leq C\|T\|_{\vec{X}\to X_{\text{weak}}}\Big\|\prod_{j=1}^{m}\langle f_{j}\rangle_{Q}\ind_{Q^{\prime}}\Big\|_{X_{\text{weak}}}

\displaystyle\leq C^{2}\|T\|^{2}_{\vec{X}\to X_{\text{weak}}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\|f_{j}\|_{X_{j}}.

The Fatou property of the
X_{j}
now extends this result to all
\vec{f}\in\vec{X}
, as desired. Now, the final assertion follows from Proposition
3.1
in the case
n=1
.
∎

To prove Theorem
6.16
, we will actually prove a more general result for matrix-weighted Banach function spaces. Given a
\mathcal{V}
-directional quasi-Banach function space
\mathbf{X}
over
\Omega
, following
[
Nie24a
]
, we define
\mathbf{X}_{\text{weak}}
as those
f\in L^{0}(\Omega;\mathcal{V})
for which

\|f\|_{\mathbf{X}_{\text{weak}}}:=\sup_{u\in\mathcal{V}}\|\ind_{\{x\in\Omega:u\in\mathcal{K}(f)(x)\}}u\|_{\mathbf{X}}<\infty.

Note that for any
E\subseteq\Omega
,
v\in\mathcal{V}
, and
f(x):=\ind_{E}(x)v
,
u\in\mathcal{H}
satisfies

u\in\mathcal{K}(f)(x)

whenever
x\in E
and
u=\lambda v
with
|\lambda|\leq 1
, which shows that

\|\ind_{E}v\|_{\mathbf{X}_{\text{weak}}}=\sup_{|\lambda|\leq 1}\|\ind_{E}\lambda v\|_{\mathbf{X}}=\|\ind_{E}v\|_{\mathbf{X}}.

Our general result is as follows:

Theorem 6.16
.

Let
\vec{X}
be
m
Banach function spaces over
\mathbf{R}^{d}
with the Fatou property and
X:=\prod_{j=1}^{m}X_{j}
. Let
\vec{W}
be matrix weights for which
\ind_{Q}\|W_{j}^{-1}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}\to\mathcal{H}_{j}}\in X_{j}^{\prime}
for all cubes
Q
for
j=1,\ldots,m
. If
T
is a non-degenerate
m
-linear Calderón–Zygmund operator satisfying

\widetilde{T}:\vec{X}_{\vec{W}}\to X_{\mathbf{W}},

then

\sup_{Q}\|T_{Q}\|_{\vec{X}_{\vec{W}}\to X_{\mathbf{W}}}\lesssim_{\vec{n},K_{X}}C^{2}\|\widetilde{T}\|_{\vec{X}_{\vec{W}}\to(X_{\mathbf{W}})_{\emph{weak}}}\|\widetilde{T}\|_{\vec{X}_{\vec{W}}\to X_{\mathbf{W}}}.

The reason we need to assume the strong-type bound for for
\widetilde{T}
is because, in general, the space
(X_{\mathbf{W}})_{\text{weak}}
is not a vector space. However, this is needed when applying the directional non-degeneracy property
2
.

For the proof of the theorem, we require several lemmata.

Lemma 6.17
.

Let
T
be a directionally non-degenerate
m
-linear Calderón–Zygmund operator. Then
T
is non-degenerate with the same constant
C
and cubes
Q^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Let
Q
be a cube and let
Q^{\prime}
be as in the definition of directional non-degeneracy. Let
u_{j}\in\mathcal{H}_{j}
be unit vectors for
j=1,\ldots,m
, and note that for any
\vec{h}\in L^{\vec{\infty}}_{c}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
we have

\widetilde{T}(h_{1}u_{1},\ldots,h_{m}u_{m})=T(\vec{h})u,

where
u:=\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}u_{j}
. Thus, property
2
of directional non-degeneracy implies that there is an
S:Q^{\prime}\times Q^{m}\to\mathbf{C}
with
|S(x,\vec{y})|\leq|Q|^{-m}
for which for all non-negative
\vec{h}\in L^{\vec{\infty}}_{c}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
we have

\prod_{j=1}^{m}\langle h_{j}\rangle_{Q}u=\tfrac{1}{2}CT(\vec{h})(x)u+\tfrac{1}{2}\Big(\int_{Q^{m}}\!S(x,\vec{y})\prod_{j=1}^{m}h_{j}(y_{j})\,\mathrm{d}\vec{y}\Big)u

for a.e.
x\in Q^{\prime}
. Hence, since
|S(x,\vec{y})|\leq|Q|^{-m}
,

\prod_{j=1}^{m}\langle h_{j}\rangle_{Q}\ind_{Q^{\prime}}\leq\tfrac{1}{2}C|T(\vec{h})(x)|+\tfrac{1}{2}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\langle h_{j}\rangle_{Q}\ind_{Q^{\prime}},

so

\prod_{j=1}^{m}\langle h_{j}\rangle_{Q}\ind_{Q^{\prime}}\leq C|T(\vec{h})(x)|,

proving that
T
satisfies property
2
of non-degeneracy, as asserted.
∎

The following lemma is a multilinear analogue of
[
Gol03
, Proposition 5.3]
:

Lemma 6.18
.

Let
Q,Q^{\prime}
be cubes in
\mathbf{R}^{d}
, let
\vec{X}
be Banach function spaces over
\mathbf{R}^{d}
, and let
\vec{W}
be matrix weights. Suppose
\ind_{Q}u\in(X_{j}^{\prime})_{W_{j}^{-1}}
for all
u\in\mathcal{H}_{j}
for
j=1,\ldots,m
, and suppose
\ind_{Q^{\prime}}u\in X_{\mathbf{W}}
for all
u\in\mathcal{H}
. If
S:Q^{\prime}\times Q^{m}\to\mathbf{C}
satisfies

|S(x,\vec{y})|\leq|Q|^{-m},

then the operator

L(\vec{f})(x):=\ind_{Q^{\prime}}(x)\int_{Q^{m}}S(x,\vec{y})\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}f_{j}(y_{j})\,\mathrm{d}\vec{y}

satisfies

\|L\|_{\vec{X}_{\vec{W}}\to X_{\mathbf{W}}}\leq C_{\vec{n},K_{X}}\|T_{Q,Q^{\prime}}\|_{\vec{X}_{\vec{W}}\to X_{\mathbf{W}}}.

Proof.

Let
\vec{f}\in\vec{X}_{\vec{W}}
of norm one, and let
\mathcal{B}_{j}
be an orthonormal basis of
\mathcal{H}_{j}
for
j=1,\ldots,m
. For any
x\in Q^{\prime}
we have

\displaystyle\Big\|\int_{Q^{m}}S(\cdot,\vec{y})

\displaystyle\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}W_{j}(x)f_{j}(y_{j})\,\mathrm{d}\vec{y}\Big\|_{\mathcal{H}}\leq\prod_{j=1}^{m}\langle\|W_{j}(x)f_{j}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}\rangle_{Q}

\displaystyle\leq|Q|^{-m}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\|\|W_{j}(x)W_{j}^{-1}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}\to\mathcal{H}_{j}}\ind_{Q}\|_{X^{\prime}_{j}}\|\|W_{j}f_{j}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}\|_{X_{j}}

\displaystyle\leq|Q|^{-m}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\sum_{e_{j}\in\mathcal{B}_{j}}\|\|W_{j}^{-1}W_{j}(x)e_{j}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}\ind_{Q}\|_{X^{\prime}_{j}}

\displaystyle\leq|Q|^{-m}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\sum_{e_{j}\in\mathcal{B}_{j}}\|A_{(X_{j}^{\prime})_{W_{j}^{-1}},Q}W_{j}(x)e_{j}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}

\displaystyle\leq n|Q|^{-m}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\|W_{j}(x)A_{(X_{j}^{\prime})_{W_{j}^{-1}},Q}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}\to\mathcal{H}_{j}}

\displaystyle=n|Q|^{-m}\Big\|\mathbf{W}\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}A_{(X_{j}^{\prime})_{W_{j}^{-1}},Q}\Big\|_{\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{H}}.

Thus, it follows from the ideal property of
X
and Lemma
2.6
that

\displaystyle\|L(\vec{f})\|_{X_{\mathbf{W}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{n}|Q|^{-m}\Big\|\ind_{Q^{\prime}}\Big\|\mathbf{W}\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}A_{(X_{j}^{\prime})_{W_{j}^{-1}},Q}\Big\|_{\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{H}}\Big\|_{X}

\displaystyle\eqsim_{n,K_{X}}|Q|^{-m}\Big\|A_{X_{\mathbf{W}},Q^{\prime}}\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}A_{(X_{j}^{\prime})_{W_{j}^{-1}},Q}\Big\|_{\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{H}}.

Thus, the result follows from Proposition
3.1
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
6.16
.

Set
\mathbf{X}_{j}:=(X_{j})_{W_{j}}
,
\mathbf{X}:=X_{\mathbf{W}}
. We will first show that for all cubes
Q
we have
\ind_{Q}u_{j}\in\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\prime}
for all
u_{j}\in\mathcal{H}_{j}
for
j=1,\ldots,m
, and
\ind_{Q}u\in\mathbf{X}
for all
u\in\mathcal{H}
. For the former result, note that per assumption

\ind_{Q}\|W_{j}^{-1}u_{j}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}\leq\ind_{Q}\|W_{j}^{-1}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}\to\mathcal{H}_{j}}\|u_{j}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}\in X_{j}^{\prime}

and hence, by the ideal property of
X_{j}^{\prime}
,
\ind_{Q}u_{j}\in\mathbf{X}_{j}^{\prime}=(X_{j}^{\prime})_{W_{j}^{-1}}
for all
u_{j}\in\mathcal{H}_{j}
,
j=1,\ldots,m
. For the latter result, Let
u_{j}\in\mathcal{H}_{j}\backslash\{0\}
for
j=1,\ldots,m
, set
u:=\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}u_{j}
, and note that by
[
Nie24a
, Section 3.2]
the spaces
(\mathbf{X}_{j})_{u_{j}}
and
\mathbf{X}_{u}
, defined by the
h_{j}\in L^{0}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
for which
h_{j}u_{j}\in\mathbf{X}_{j}
for
j=1,\ldots,m
and
h\in L^{0}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
for which
hu\in\mathbf{X}
respectively, are Banach function spaces over
\mathbf{R}^{d}
.

Noting that

(6.1)

\widetilde{T}(h_{1}u_{1},\ldots,h_{m}u_{m})=T(\vec{h})u,

the boundedness
\widetilde{T}:\vec{\mathbf{X}}\to\mathbf{X}
implies that
T:\vec{\mathbf{X}}_{\vec{u}}\to\mathbf{X}_{u}
. Thus, since
T
is non-degenerate by Lemma
6.17
, it follows from Proposition
6.15
that
\ind_{Q}\in\mathbf{X}_{u}
, i.e.,
\ind_{Q}u\in\mathbf{X}
, for all cubes
Q
, as desired.

Next, since
\|\ind_{E}u\|_{\mathbf{X}}=\|\ind_{E}u\|_{\mathbf{X}_{\text{weak}}}
for all
E\subseteq\mathbf{R}^{d}
and
u\in\mathcal{H}
, it follows from
1
and (
6.1
) that for all
\vec{u}\in\vec{\mathcal{H}}
and
0\leq h_{1},\ldots,h_{m}\in L^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
with
\prod_{j=1}^{m}h_{j}=\ind_{Q^{\prime}}
we have

\ind_{Q}\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}u_{j}\in C\mathcal{K}(\widetilde{T}(h_{1}u_{1},\ldots,h_{m}u_{m}))

a.e. and, hence, by the directional ideal property of
\mathbf{X}_{\text{weak}}
,

\displaystyle\Big\|\ind_{Q}\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}u_{j}\Big\|_{\mathbf{X}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\widetilde{T}(h_{1}u_{1},\ldots,h_{m}u_{m})\|_{\mathbf{X}_{\text{weak}}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\widetilde{T}\|_{\vec{\mathbf{X}}\to\mathbf{X}_{\text{weak}}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\|h_{j}\|W_{j}u_{j}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}\|_{X_{j}}.

By the ideal property of the
X_{j}
, this is true for all
0\leq h_{j}\|W_{j}u_{j}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}\in X_{j}
with
\ind_{Q^{\prime}}\leq\prod_{j=1}^{m}h_{j}
. Hence, taking an infimum over all possible
h_{j}
, we conclude that

(6.2)

\begin{split}\Big\|\ind_{Q}\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}u_{j}\Big\|_{\mathbf{X}}&\leq C\|\widetilde{T}\|_{\vec{X}_{\vec{W}}\to\mathbf{X}_{\text{weak}}}\|\ind_{Q^{\prime}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\|W_{j}u_{j}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}\|_{X}\\
&=C\|\widetilde{T}\|_{\vec{X}_{\vec{W}}\to\mathbf{X}_{\text{weak}}}\Big\|\ind_{Q^{\prime}}\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}u_{j}\Big\|_{\mathbf{X}}.\end{split}

Now, by
2
, for each
0<\eta<1
we can find an
S:Q^{\prime}\times Q^{m}\to\mathbf{C}
with
|S(x,\vec{y})|\leq|Q|^{-m}
a.e., and, defining
L
as in Lemma
6.18
,

(6.3)

\begin{split}\|T_{Q,Q^{\prime}}(\vec{f})\|_{\mathbf{X}}&\leq(1-\alpha)CK_{X}\|\widetilde{T}(\ind_{Q}\vec{f})\|_{\mathbf{X}}+\alpha K_{X}\|L(\ind_{Q}\vec{f})\|_{\mathbf{X}}\\
&\leq(1-\alpha)CK_{X}\|\widetilde{T}\|_{\vec{\mathbf{X}}\to\mathbf{X}}+\alpha K_{X}C_{\vec{n},K_{X}}\|T_{Q,Q^{\prime}}\|_{\vec{\mathbf{X}}\to\mathbf{X}}\end{split}

for all
\vec{f}\in L^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\vec{\mathcal{H}})\cap\vec{\mathbf{X}}
with
\|f_{j}\|_{\mathbf{X}_{j}}\leq 1
for
j=1,\ldots,m
. For arbitrary
\vec{f}\in\vec{\mathbf{X}}
of norm bounded by
1
, define

h_{j}^{N}:=\ind_{\{x\in\mathbf{R}^{d}:|x|\leq N,\,\|f_{j}(x)\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}\leq N\}}

and set
f_{j,N}:=h_{j}^{N}f_{j}\in L^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\mathcal{H}_{j})\cap\mathbf{X}_{j}
for positive integers
N
and
j=1,\ldots,m
. Then

\ind_{Q^{\prime}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\|W_{j}(\cdot)\langle f_{j,N}\rangle_{Q}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}\leq\ind_{Q^{\prime}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\langle\|W_{j}(\cdot)f_{j}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{j}}\rangle_{Q},

so by the dominated convergence theorem and the Fatou property of
X=\prod_{j=1}^{m}X_{j}
(see
[
Sch10
]
), we may conclude that (
6.3
) actually holds for all
\vec{f}\in\vec{\mathbf{X}}
of norm bounded by
1
, i.e.,

\|T_{Q,Q^{\prime}}\|_{\vec{\mathbf{X}}\to\mathbf{X}}\leq(1-\alpha)CK_{X}\|\widetilde{T}\|_{\vec{\mathbf{X}}\to\mathbf{X}}+\alpha K_{X}C_{\vec{n},K_{X}}\|T_{Q,Q^{\prime}}\|_{\vec{\mathbf{X}}\to\mathbf{X}}.

Choosing
\alpha=\tfrac{1}{2}K_{X}^{-1}C_{\vec{n},K_{X}}^{-1}
and using (
6.2
), we conclude that

\|T_{Q}\|_{\vec{\mathbf{X}}\to\mathbf{X}}\leq C\|\widetilde{T}\|_{\vec{\mathbf{X}}\to\mathbf{X}_{\text{weak}}}\|T_{Q,Q^{\prime}}\|_{\vec{\mathbf{X}}\to\mathbf{X}}\lesssim_{\vec{n},K_{X}}C^{2}\|\widetilde{T}\|_{\vec{\mathbf{X}}\to\mathbf{X}_{\text{weak}}}\|\widetilde{T}\|_{\vec{\mathbf{X}}\to\mathbf{X}}.

Taking a supremum over all cubes
Q
proves the assertion.
∎

Proof of Theorem
6.13
.

We apply Theorem
6.16
with
X_{j}=L^{p_{j}}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
. The assertion then follows from the definition of
[\vec{W}]_{\vec{p}}
.
∎

As our non-degeneracy condition is quite convoluted, we provide an example.

Example 6.19
.

Let
T
be the
m
-linear Riesz transform of the first coordinate, i.e., the
m
-linear Calderón–Zygmund operator associated to the kernel

K(x,\vec{y})=\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{m}(x^{1}-y_{j}^{1})}{\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{m}|x-y_{j}|\Big)^{md+1}},

where
x\in\mathbf{R}^{d}
,
\vec{y}=(y_{1},\ldots,y_{m})\in(\mathbf{R}^{d})^{m}
, and where
x=(x^{1},\ldots,x^{d})
,
y_{j}=(y_{j}^{1},\ldots,y_{j}^{m})
denote the coordinates of
x,y_{j}\in\mathbf{R}^{d}
,
j=1,\ldots,m
.

Given a cube
Q
in
\mathbf{R}^{d}
, set

Q^{\prime}:=Q+2m\ell(Q)e_{1},

where
e_{1}=(1,0,\ldots,0)\in\mathbf{R}^{d}
. To check
1
, let
0\leq h_{1},\ldots,h_{m}\in L^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
supported in
Q^{\prime}
with
\prod_{j=1}^{m}h_{j}=\ind_{Q^{\prime}}
. Since for
x\in Q
and
\vec{y}\in(Q^{\prime})^{m}
we have

(2m-1)\ell(Q)\leq y_{j}^{1}-x^{1}\leq(2m+1)\ell(Q)

and

0\leq y_{j}^{k}-x^{k}\leq\ell(Q)

for all
k\in\{2,\ldots,d\}
,
j\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
, it follows that for

C_{m,d}:=\frac{(d-1+(2m+1)^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}}{m(2m-1)}

we have

\displaystyle\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{m}|x-y_{j}|\Big)^{md+1}

\displaystyle\leq m(2m-1)C_{m,d}\ell(Q)^{md+1}=C_{m,d}|Q|^{m}m(2m-1)\ell(Q)

\displaystyle\leq C_{m,d}|Q|^{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}(y_{j}^{1}-x^{1}).

Thus, since
|Q|=|Q^{\prime}|
,

\displaystyle C_{m,d}|T(\vec{h})(x)|

\displaystyle=C_{m,d}\int_{(Q^{\prime})^{m}}\!\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{m}(y_{j}^{1}-x^{1})}{\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{m}|x-y_{j}|\Big)^{md+1}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}h_{j}(y_{j})\,\mathrm{d}\vec{y}

\displaystyle\geq\prod_{j=1}^{m}\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ^{\prime}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q^{\prime}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q^{\prime}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q^{\prime}}}\!h_{j}(y)\,\mathrm{d}y\geq\Big(\mathchoice{\mathop{\kern 1.99997pt\vrule width=6.00006pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.49997pt\kern-8.00003pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.27773ptQ^{\prime}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q^{\prime}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q^{\prime}}}{\mathop{\kern 1.00006pt\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.59996pt\kern-6.00006pt\intop}\nolimits_{Q^{\prime}}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}h_{j}(y)^{\frac{1}{m}}\,\mathrm{d}y\Big)^{m}=1

for a.e.
x\in Q
, as desired.

For
2
, let
\vec{f}\in L^{\vec{\infty}}_{c}(\mathbf{R}^{d};\vec{\mathcal{H}})
be supported in
Q
and let
0<\alpha<1
. Then we have

\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}\langle f_{j}\rangle_{Q}=(1-\alpha)C_{m,d}\widetilde{T}(\vec{f})(x)+\alpha\int_{Q^{m}}\!S(x,\vec{y})\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}f_{j}(y_{j})\,\mathrm{d}\vec{y},

where

S(x,\vec{y})=\alpha^{-1}\big(|Q|^{-m}-(1-\alpha)C_{m,d}K(x,\vec{y})\big).

If
x\in Q^{\prime}
and
\vec{y}\in Q^{m}
, then by a similar computation as above,

K(x,\vec{y})=\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{m}(x^{1}-y_{j}^{1})}{\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{m}|x-y_{j}|\Big)^{md+1}}\geq C_{m,d}^{-1}|Q|^{-m}.

Hence, we have

\alpha|S(x,\vec{y})|=|Q|^{-m}-(1-\alpha)C_{m,d}K(x,\vec{y})\leq(1-(1-\alpha))|Q|^{-m}=\alpha|Q|^{-m},

proving the desired result.

7.
Proof of the main result

Proof of Theorem
A
.

The implications
3
\Rightarrow
1
&
2
correspond to
1
&
2
of Theorem
B
respectively. The implication
2
\Rightarrow
3
follows from Proposition
5.1
, and the implication
1
\Rightarrow
3
from Theorem
6.13
applied with
T
being the multilinear Riesz transform of Example
6.19
.
∎

Appendix A

Here we give the details of the proofs of Propositions
6.1
,
6.2
and
6.3
. We consider a
m
-linear Calderón–Zygmund operator with a kernel
K
having a modulus of continuity
\omega
satisfying the Dini condition. We denote by
C_{K}>0
the best constant in the boundedness and smoothness conditions for the kernel
K
. Moreover, we denote by
D
the doubling constant of
\omega
.

Proposition A.1
.

The operator
T
admits a bounded extension
L^{\vec{1}}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\to L^{\frac{1}{m},\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
with

\|T\|_{L^{\vec{1}}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\to L^{\frac{1}{m},\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{d})}\leq C(\|T\|_{L^{\vec{q}}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\to L^{q}(\mathbf{R}^{d})}+C_{K}\|\omega\|_{\mathrm{Dini}}),

where the constant
C>0
depends only on
d,m,\vec{q}
and the doubling constant of
\omega
.

Proof.

We adapt the proof of
[
DHL18
, Lemma 6.1]
. Let
\mathcal{D}
be the standard dyadic grid in
\mathbf{R}^{d}
. Let
\vec{f}=(f_{1},\ldots,f_{m})
be a
m
-tuple of functions that are finite linear combinations with complex coefficients of characteristic functions of cubes in
\mathcal{D}
. Let
\lambda>0
be arbitrary. We show that

\lambda|\{|T(\vec{f})|>\lambda\}|^{m}\leq C(\|T\|_{L^{\vec{q}}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\to L^{q}(\mathbf{R}^{d})}+C_{K}\|\omega\|_{\mathrm{Dini}}),

where the constant
C>0
depends only on
d,m,\vec{q}
and the doubling constant of
\omega
. Without loss of generality, we may assume that for every
j=1,\ldots,m
it is not the case that
f_{j}=0
a.e. on
\mathbf{R}^{d}
.

We consider positive real numbers
a_{1},\ldots,a_{m}
to be chosen below. Then, for each
j=1,\ldots,m
, we consider a Calderón–Zygmund decomposition of
f_{j}
at height
\alpha_{j}\lambda
. Thus, we obtain functions measurable functions
g_{j},b_{j}:\mathbf{R}^{d}\to\mathbf{C}
, a finite family
\mathcal{F}_{j}
of pairwise disjoint cubes in
\mathbf{R}^{d}
and functions
b_{j,Q}:\mathbf{R}^{d}\to\mathbf{C}
,
Q\in\mathcal{F}_{j}
with the following properties:

\displaystyle f_{j}=g_{j}+b_{j},

\displaystyle b_{j}=\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{F}_{j}}b_{j,Q},

\displaystyle\|g_{j}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbf{R}^{d})}\leq\|f_{j}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbf{R}^{d})},\quad\|g_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{d})}\leq 2^{d}\alpha_{j}\lambda,

\displaystyle b_{j,Q}\text{ vanishes outside }Q\text{ and }\int_{Q}b_{j,Q}(x)\mathrm{d}x=0,\penalty\ \|b_{j,Q}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbf{R}^{d})}\leq 2^{d+1}\alpha_{j}\lambda|Q|\text{ for each }Q\in\mathcal{F},

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{F}_{j}}|Q|\leq\frac{1}{\alpha_{j}\lambda}\|f_{j}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbf{R}^{d})}.

Set
\Omega_{j}:=\bigcup_{Q\in\mathcal{F}_{j}}(3Q)
for each
j=1,\ldots,m
. Set also
E:=\mathbf{R}^{d}\setminus\Big(\bigcup_{j=1}^{m}\Omega_{j}\Big)
. Then, we have

\displaystyle|\{|T(\vec{f})|>\lambda\}|\leq\sum_{j=1}^{m}|\Omega_{j}|+\sum_{\vec{h}\in\{g,b\}^{m}}\Big|\Big\{x\in E:\penalty\ |T(h_{1},\ldots,h_{m})|>\frac{\lambda}{2^{m}}\Big\}\Big|.

We estimate each term separately. First of all, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{m}|\Omega_{j}|\leq\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{F}_{j}}|3Q|\lesssim_{d}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{F}_{j}}|Q|\leq\frac{1}{\lambda}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{1}{a_{j}}\|f_{j}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbf{R}^{d})}.

We turn our attention to the other
2^{m}
terms. For the term where only “good” functions appear, we have

\displaystyle\Big|\Big\{x\in E:\penalty\ |T(\vec{g})|>\frac{\lambda}{2^{m}}\Big\}\Big|\leq\Big(\frac{2^{m}}{\lambda}\Big)^{q}\|T(\vec{g})\|_{L^{q}(\mathbf{R}^{d})}^{q}\leq\Big(\frac{2^{m}}{\lambda}\Big)^{q}\|T\|_{L^{\vec{q}}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\to L^{q}(\mathbf{R}^{d})}^{q}\|\vec{g}\|_{L^{\vec{q}}(\mathbf{R}^{d})}^{q}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{d,\vec{q}}\Big(\frac{2^{m}}{\lambda}\Big)^{q}\|T\|_{L^{\vec{q}}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\to L^{q}(\mathbf{R}^{d})}^{q}\Big(\prod_{j=1}^{m}(a_{j}\lambda)^{\frac{1}{q_{j}^{\prime}}}\Big)\Big(\prod_{j=1}^{m}\|f_{j}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbf{R}^{d})}^{\frac{1}{q_{j}}}\Big)^{q}.

Next, we estimate the terms where there is a mixture of “good” and “bad” functions, or where there are only “bad” functions. Let
\vec{h}\in\{g,b\}^{m}
such that it is not the case that
h_{j}=g_{j}
for all
j=1,\ldots,m
. Let
B:=\{j\in\{1,\ldots,m\}:\penalty\ h_{j}=b_{j}\}
and
A:=\{1,\ldots,m\}\setminus B
. Consider enumerations
A:=\{s_{1},\ldots,s_{n}\}
and
B:=\{r_{1},\ldots,r_{\ell}\}
. We abbreviate
\mathrm{d}y_{A}:=\mathrm{d}y_{s_{1}}\ldots\mathrm{d}y_{s_{n}}
and
\mathrm{d}y_{B}:=\mathrm{d}y_{r_{1}}\ldots\mathrm{d}y_{r_{\ell}}
. Then, for each
x\in E
we have

\displaystyle|T(\vec{h})(x)|=\Big|\int_{(\mathbf{R}^{d})^{m}}K(x,\vec{y})\prod_{j=1}^{m}h_{j}(y_{j})\,\mathrm{d}\vec{y}\Big|

\displaystyle=\int_{(\mathbf{R}^{d})^{A}}\Big(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q_{t}\in\mathcal{F}_{r_{t}}\\
t=1,\ldots,\ell\end{subarray}}\int_{Q_{1}\times\ldots\times Q_{\ell}}K(x,\vec{y})\prod_{j\in B}b_{j,Q_{j}}(y_{j})\mathrm{d}y_{B}\Big)\prod_{j\in A}g_{j}(y_{j})\mathrm{d}y_{A}

\displaystyle=\int_{(\mathbf{R}^{d})^{A}}\bigg(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q_{t}\in\mathcal{F}_{r_{t}}\\
t=1,\ldots,\ell\end{subarray}}\int_{Q_{1}\times\ldots\times Q_{\ell}}(K(x,\vec{y})-K(x,y_{1},\ldots,y_{r_{1}-1},c_{Q_{1}},y_{r_{1}+1},\ldots,y_{m}))

\displaystyle\times\prod_{j\in B}b_{j,Q_{j}}(y_{j})\mathrm{d}y_{B}\bigg)\prod_{j\in A}g_{j}(y_{j})\mathrm{d}y_{A}

\displaystyle\leq C_{K}\int_{(\mathbf{R}^{d})^{A}}\bigg(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q_{t}\in\mathcal{F}_{r_{t}}\\
t=1,\ldots,\ell\end{subarray}}\int_{Q_{1}\times\ldots\times Q_{\ell}}\omega\Big(\frac{|y_{r_{1}}-c_{Q_{1}}|}{\sum_{j=1}^{m}|x-y_{j}|}\Big)\frac{1}{\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{m}|x-y_{j}|\Big)^{md}}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{j\in B}b_{j,Q_{j}}(y_{j})\mathrm{d}y_{B}\bigg)\prod_{j\in A}g_{j}(y_{j})\mathrm{d}y_{A}

\displaystyle=C_{K}\int_{(\mathbf{R}^{d})^{A}}\bigg(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q_{t}\in\mathcal{F}_{r_{t}}\\
t=1,\ldots,\ell\end{subarray}}\int_{Q_{1}\times\ldots\times Q_{\ell}}\omega\Big(\frac{|y_{r_{1}}-c_{Q_{1}}|}{\sum_{j\in A}|y_{j}|+\sum_{j\in B}|x-y_{j}|}\Big)

\displaystyle\times\frac{1}{\Big(\sum_{j\in A}|y_{j}|+\sum_{j\in B}|x-y_{j}|\Big)^{md}}\prod_{j\in B}b_{j,Q_{j}}(y_{j})\mathrm{d}y_{B}\bigg)\prod_{j\in A}g_{j}(y_{j})\mathrm{d}y_{A}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{m,d,D}C_{K}\int_{(\mathbf{R}^{d})^{A}}\bigg(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q_{t}\in\mathcal{F}_{r_{t}}\\
t=1,\ldots,\ell\end{subarray}}\int_{Q_{1}\times\ldots\times Q_{\ell}}\omega\Big(\frac{\ell(Q_{1})}{\sum_{j\in A}|y_{j}|+\sum_{j\in B}|x-c_{Q_{j}}|}\Big)

\displaystyle\times\frac{1}{\Big(\sum_{j\in A}|y_{j}|+\sum_{j\in B}|x-c_{Q_{j}}|\Big)^{md}}\prod_{j\in B}b_{j,Q_{j}}(y_{j})\mathrm{d}y_{B}\bigg)\prod_{j\in A}g_{j}(y_{j})\mathrm{d}y_{A}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{d,m}C_{K}\Big(\prod_{j\in A}a_{j}\Big)\lambda^{\#A}\int_{(\mathbf{R}^{d})^{A}}\bigg(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q_{t}\in\mathcal{F}_{r_{t}}\\
t=1,\ldots,\ell\end{subarray}}\int_{Q_{1}\times\ldots\times Q_{\ell}}\omega\Big(\frac{\ell(Q_{1})}{\sum_{j\in A}|y_{j}|+\sum_{j\in B}|x-c_{Q_{j}}|}\Big)

\displaystyle\times\frac{1}{\Big(\sum_{j\in A}|y_{j}|+\sum_{j\in B}|x-c_{Q_{j}}|\Big)^{md}}\prod_{j\in B}b_{j,Q_{j}}(y_{j})\mathrm{d}y_{B}\bigg)\mathrm{d}y_{A}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{d,m}C_{K}\Big(\prod_{j=1}^{m}a_{j}\Big)\lambda^{m}\int_{(\mathbf{R}^{d})^{A}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q_{t}\in\mathcal{F}_{r_{t}}\\
t=1,\ldots,\ell\end{subarray}}\Big(\prod_{k=1}^{\ell}|Q_{k}|\Big)\omega\Big(\frac{\ell(Q_{1})}{\sum_{j\in A}|y_{j}|+\sum_{j\in B}|x-c_{Q_{j}}|}\Big)

\displaystyle\times\frac{1}{\Big(\sum_{j\in A}|y_{j}|+\sum_{j\in B}|x-c_{Q_{j}}|\Big)^{md}}\mathrm{d}y_{A}

\displaystyle\leq C_{K}\Big(\prod_{j=1}^{m}a_{j}\Big)\lambda^{m}\int_{(\mathbf{R}^{d})^{A}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q_{t}\in\mathcal{F}_{r_{t}}\\
t=1,\ldots,\ell\end{subarray}}\Big(\prod_{k=1}^{\ell}|Q_{k}|\Big)\omega\Big(\frac{\ell(Q_{1})}{\sum_{j\in B}|x-c_{Q_{j}}|}\Big)

\displaystyle\times\frac{1}{\Big(\sum_{j\in A}|y_{j}|+\sum_{j\in B}|x-c_{Q_{j}}|\Big)^{md}}\mathrm{d}y_{A}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{m,d}C_{K}\Big(\prod_{j=1}^{m}a_{j}\Big)\lambda^{m}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q_{t}\in\mathcal{F}_{r_{t}}\\
t=1,\ldots,\ell\end{subarray}}\Big(\prod_{k=1}^{\ell}|Q_{k}|\Big)\omega\Big(\frac{\ell(Q_{1})}{\sum_{j\in B}|x-c_{Q_{j}}|}\Big)\frac{1}{\Big(\sum_{j\in B}|x-c_{Q_{j}}|\Big)^{(m-\#A)d}}
